## I. Report to Shareholders

We sincerely appreciate the ongoing care, support, and love that everyone has shown towards Launch. It is our hope that you will continue to follow and support us in the future, and for this, we extend our heartfelt thanks.

We hereby report the business results of Launch for the fiscal year 2022 and the outline of the business plan for the fiscal year 2023 as follows:

## I. Business Report for the Fiscal Year 2022

- (1) Results of Implementation of 2022 Annual Business Plan

The main business of Launch in the fiscal year 2022 was the export of golf clubs, with a full-year operating revenue of NTD 3,598,019 thousand, an increase of 38% compared to year 2021. Net profit after tax for the period was NT$588,731 thousand, and earnings per share after tax were NT$11.55.

- (2) Budget Execution in 2022

The company did not disclose any financial forecast for the year 2022, thus there is no budget execution information available.

## (3) Financial Revenue and Expense Analysis

## (4) Research and development

- (1) Development of large-area transfer printing technology and new products.
- (2) Development and application of high-performance materials and products.
- (3) Mold processing technology and independent design capability.

## II. Summary of the 2023 Operating Plan

- (1) Business Objectives and Implementation Overview
- (1) Continuously enhance factory competitiveness by strengthening automation, intelligence, and smartness.
- (2) Enhance quality and operational efficiency by integrating digital management mechanisms.
- (3) Boost R&amp;D capabilities to seize new business opportunities and develop high-value-added products.
- (4) Foster a passionate environment and uphold core values of sincerity, creativity, and satisfaction, becoming the most trusted partner for employees and customers.
- (5) Implement sustainable development goals, create a friendly working environment, cultivate talent, prioritize employee welfare and shareholder interests, and generate shared value.

## (2) Important Sales and Production Strategies and Expectations

- (1) Strengthen digital management mechanisms to enhance production efficiency and operational effectiveness.
- (2) Connect with external strategic partners to ensure smooth supply and delivery.
- (3) Make steady investments to meet customer order demands.
- (4) Enhance advanced product development capabilities and shorten the time to introduce mass production.
- (5) Implement quality management systems to strengthen quality control and reduce internal defect rates and external customer complaints.
- (6) The sales volume for the year 2023 is expected to continue to exceed that of 2022.

## (3) Company's Future Development Strategies

- (1) Continuously expand the sales proportion of high-priced golf balls and enhance the product's added value.
- (2) Automate and digitize various production processes to improve product quality and reduce costs.
- (3) Extend the core process application capabilities and explore new business opportunities.
- (4) Strengthen talent recruitment, training, utilization, and retention to establish a talent pool that is competitive in the future.

## (4) Impact of External Competitive, Regulatory, and Macro-Economic Environmentsenvironment

As golf is an outdoor leisure sport that can be played with proper social distancing, the number of people playing golf worldwide reached a historic high of 66 million in 2021. However, in the post-pandemic era after 2022, due to the sharp rise in inflation in countries such as the United States and Europe, the US Federal Reserve has rapidly raised interest rates since March to curb inflation,  leading  to  increased  volatility  in  global  financial  markets.  Despite concerns of a recessionary business climate after the interest rate hikes, the golf consumer group is mainly composed of middle and high-income individuals who are less sensitive to price, thus maintaining strong purchasing power in the end consumer market. In addition, due to the impact of a strong US dollar, the New Taiwan Dollar has significantly depreciated, leading to improved revenue and profitability for the company.

Looking  ahead  to  2023,  most  investment  research  institutions  expect  the economic growth rate to slow significantly compared to 2022. Although the golf consumer market may be relatively less affected, risks such as rising costs of interest rates, wages, raw materials, and supply chain disruptions will still affect business operations. The company will also be prepared to respond to the changing situation. With the issue of climate change spreading, the increase of uncontrollable risks brought by the epidemic and geopolitical issues, and society's  increasing  emphasis  on  ESG  issues  such  as  environmental  protection, especially with the pressure to achieve global carbon emission reduction by half by 2030 at the United Nations Climate Summit, the company will include green  energy  as  an  option  for  future  energy  use,  and  continue  to  implement energy conservation, waste reduction, and waste recycling to comply with the

international  trend  of  sustainable  development  and  maintain  its  own  market competitiveness.

Board ：

Liu, An-Hao

## II. Company Introduction

## I. Date of Establishment

July 28, 2006

## II. Company History

- July 2006:  Established with a capital of NT$150,000 thousand.
- November 2006: Increased capital to NT$350,000 thousand to accommodate business growth.
- June 2007:  Established a new production line (NPS) with an annual output of 1 million dozen and applied for two patents.
- April 2008: Completed Callaway's new product trial production (Pilot Run).
- June 2008:  Developed extrusion machines and hot pressing molds to improve production efficiency by 48%.
- October 2008: Implemented the SAP ERP system to integrate enterprise management information.
- December 2008: Completed equipment expansion plan and introduced the industry's first high-speed injection molding machine using thin-shell technology.
- March 2009: Developed 0.9mm thin-shell injection technology and materials.
- May 2009: Completed the UV Ink process technology development, reducing process settling time.
- July 2009: Increased capital by issuing 3,500,000 new shares of common stock for a total of NTD 385,000 thousand.
- May 2010: Purchased a factory in the Pingtung Export Processing Zone for NTD 100,000 thousand to accommodate business expansion.
- August 2010: Increased capital by converting retained earnings of NTD 15,000 thousand and issuing  4,000  thousand  new  shares  of  common  stock  for  a  total  of  NTD 440,000 thousand.
- September 2010: Approved the public offering of company stock.
- October 2010: Listed the company's stock on the Gre Tai Securities Market.
- January 2011: We were awarded the third place in the "Kaohsiung Citizen Move for 100% Healthy Living" by the Kaohsiung City Government Health Bureau. We developed anti-cutting outer shell materials.
- March 2011: ISO 9001:2008 certification obtained.
- July 2011: Relocated from Dashu District, Kaohsiung City to the Pingtung Export Processing Zone in Pingtung City to expand operations.
- September 2011: Increased capital by converting retained earnings of NTD 13,200 thousand

for a total of NTD 453,200 thousand.

- November 2011: Received the "Landscape Design Award of Excellence" in the 2011 Economic Affairs Department's Export Processing Zone Park Business Greening Competition.
- December 2012: Obtained ISO 14001:2004 and OHSAS 18001:2007 certifications.
- February 2013: Received the "Contribution to Public Welfare Award" in the 2012 Economic Affairs  Department's  Export  Processing  Zone  Public  Welfare  Contribution Awards.
- September 2013: Successfully developed a two-layer ball with extremely soft but high rebound properties, greatly improving the feel and distance when hitting the ball.
- December 2013: Received the "Healthy Workplace Award" in the 2013 National Health Administration's Joyful Health Awards.
- Aug. 2014: Awarded the Health Promotion Mark for Workplace Certification by the Ministry of Health and Welfare, National Health Administration.
- Nov. 2014: The company's stock was listed on the Over-The-Counter market on Nov. 10, 2014.
- Nov. 2014: Cash capital increase of NTD 56,700 thousand, increasing the total capital to NTD 509,900 thousand.
- Nov. 2014: Awarded the Silver Award for Energy Conservation by the Export Processing Zone Administration, Ministry of Economic Affairs.
- Nov. 2014: Certified by the Customs Administration, Ministry of Finance, as an Authorized Economic Operator (AEO).
- Nov. 2014: Ranked 1st in the National Workplace Health Exercise Competition "Let's Show Health Together" held by the Ministry of Health and Welfare, National Health Administration.
- Jan. 2015: Completed development and launched mass production of chip short-range practice golf balls, meeting the needs of practice fields with limited space.
- Oct. 2016: Obtained ISO 9001:2015 certification.
- Nov. 2016: Completed the 2015 Corporate Social Responsibility Report.
- Nov. 2016: Selected for the "50 Small Stories Contributing to the Export Processing Zone" essay contest held by the Export Processing Zone Administration, Ministry of Economic Affairs.
- Aug. 2017: Introduced mass production of matte finish and TPU golf balls.
- December 2017: Received Outstanding Unit - Class A Award for hiring employees with disabilities in Pingtung County.
- December 2017: Obtained certification for ISO 14001:2015 revision.

- January 2018: Awarded the Health Promotion Mark by the Health Promotion Administration, Ministry of Health and Welfare.
- July 2018: Obtained building permit for the second phase of new factory construction.
- December 2018: Awarded Excellent Institution - Class A Award for hiring employees with disabilities in 2018.
- May 2019: Completed the second phase of new factory construction and obtained usage permit.
- December 2019: Awarded Outstanding Institution - Class A Award for hiring employees with disabilities in Pingtung County in 2019.
- September 2020: Replaced the original fixed pollution source control equipment with the high-efficiency zeolite rotary RTO fixed pollution source control equipment.
- December 2020: Received the Outstanding Award for Hiring People with Disabilities in Pingtung County.
- September 2021: Launched the Ice Water Machine Energy-saving Project, which achieves the goal of energy-saving by setting the temperature conditions of the ice water machine and extending the start-up time of the compressor.

December 2021: Passed the audit and verification of ISO45001 and ISO14001 management systems.

II. Board Diversity and Independence: (1) Board Diversity:

Core

Names fication

Directors

In accordance with Article 20 of the "Practical Guidelines for Corporate Governance" of our company, the selection of board members is based on the principle of selecting people based on their abilities. The composition of the board should consider diversity, including the basic composition (such as age, gender, nationality, etc.), complementary abilities across industries, and relevant skills (such as finance, accounting, law, information technology, and public welfare). In addition, no more than one-third of the board should be composed of directors who also serve as company executives. The board should possess diverse complementary abilities to make informed business judgments, effectively manage operations, provide leadership and crisis management. Required Capabilities of the Board:

- 1.Operational judgment capability.
- 2.Accounting and financial analysis capability.
- 3.Management capability.
- 4.Crisis management capability.
- 5.Industry knowledge.
- 6.International market perspective.
- 7.Leadership capability.
- 8.Decision-making capability.

In accordance with the diversified policy, on July 12, 2021, the sixth board of directors of the Company was elected, consisting of nine directors (including three independent directors). The three independent directors accounted for 33.3% of the board members, and all three independent directors had served for less than three years. The age range of board members is between 40 and 80 years old, and all are distinguished figures from the industry and academia. Among them, three members of the board of directors hold doctoral degrees, have multiple cultural backgrounds and an international perspective. In addition to possessing sufficient corporate governance and industry technical experience, the professional knowledge and skills of the directors cover multiple aspects such as industry, finance and accounting, technology, and management.

## （ II ） Independence of the board of directors:

All members of the board have not been involved in any of the circumstances listed in Article 30 of the Company Act, and the three independent directors account for 33.3% of the board members, all serving for less than three years. Among the general directors, Mr. Liu An-Hao, the Chairman of the Board, and Mr. Cheng Hsi-Chien, a director, are seconddegree relatives by marriage, and none of the other directors have circumstances as defined in paragraph 3 and paragraph 4 of Article 26-3 of the Securities and Exchange Act (i.e. where directors have more than half of the seats occupied by spouses or relatives within the second degree of kinship).

Explanations:

1. Policy for the allocation of compensation to directors and supervisors:

The remuneration of the directors and supervisors of the Company is paid in accordance with the Company's articles of association, which stipulate a distribution of no more than 5% of the profits, and is based on the overall operational performance, future business risks and development, and reference to industry standards, among other factors. The remuneration is subject to review by the Compensation Committee, approval by the Board of Directors, and subsequent distribution after reporting to the shareholders' meeting.

2. Managerial compensation distribution policy:

## (1) Policy, Standards, and Composition of Compensation

The compensation paid to our executives consists of salaries, bonuses, and employee benefits, which are determined in accordance with our company's relevant regulations.  Salaries  and  bonuses  are  distributed  after  being  reviewed  by  the Compensation Committee and approved by the Board of Directors, while employee benefits are distributed after being reported to the shareholders' meeting. The policy for paying compensation takes into account the overall performance of the company and the contribution of the managers to the company's achievements.

- (2) Procedures for Determining Compensation

The remuneration received by managers is determined based on their education, experience, years of service, job characteristics, and other factors, and is also assessed based on the company's actual operational performance and contribution each year. The methods for determining remuneration are in accordance with the company's "Salary Management Operating Procedures", "Annual and Year-end Bonus Disbursement Operating Procedures", "Performance Assessment Operating Procedures", and "Employee and Director Remuneration Disbursement Methods". The remuneration of the company's managers must be reviewed by the Remuneration Committee and approved by the Board of Directors before being disbursed.

- (3) Relationship with Management Performance and Future Risks

The remuneration of our company's managers is evaluated based on their education, experience, years of service, job characteristics, and is determined according to the actual operational performance and contribution of the company each year, while also taking into consideration the industry or market general standards, the management responsibilities they undertake, and their contribution and performance. Our company's management and remuneration committee will review and make appropriate adjustments regularly to ensure that the remuneration is sufficient to recognize their responsibilities and risks.

3. Directors and managers are paid according to their level of involvement, contribution, and industry standards. Bonuses are paid based on the company's earnings, operating performance, and industry standards. The evaluation of the compensation for directors and managers will be based on their degree of involvement in the company's operations, their individual performance contributions, and will be handled according  to  our  company's  "Employee  Remuneration  and  Director  Compensation Distribution Regulations". If there is a moral hazard event or other risk event that negatively affects the company's image, reputation, internal management, or personnel issues, the remuneration percentage will be calculated based on the achievement

of their goals, operational efficiency, and overall contribution, and will be given reasonable compensation. The system will be reviewed regularly according to the actual operating conditions and relevant laws and regulations.

3.  Communication between independent directors and the internal  audit manager and accountant (including major matters, methods, and results of communication regarding the company's financial and business conditions):
- (1) The internal audit manager shall report on the execution of internal audit business at each board meeting and submit audit reports on a monthly basis and follow-up reports on missing items on a quarterly basis to independent directors. If there are any comments on the report content and operational information, contact the internal audit manager directly to understand and exchange opinions.
- (2) The accountant shall attend each quarterly board or audit committee meeting or hold an independent meeting with independent directors, report on the company's financial condition, operating conditions, and internal control audit status to independent directors, and explain the recent impact of legal amendments on the company to the board of directors. Full communication and exchanges shall be conducted with attendees.
4. The audit committee's work priorities for the year 2022 include:

(1) Formulating or revising internal control systems in accordance with Article 14-1 of the Securities and Exchange Act.

- (2) Evaluation of the effectiveness of the internal control system.
- (3) Formulating or revising processing procedures for significant financial business activities, such  as  asset  acquisition  or  disposal,  engaging  in  derivative  commodity  transactions, lending funds to others, endorsing for others, or providing guarantees, in accordance with Article 36-1 of the Securities and Exchange Act.
- (4) Reviewing the internal audit plan.
- (5) Annual financial reports and quarterly financial reports signed or stamped by the chairman, managers, and accounting supervisor.

- Note: If any independent director resigns before the end of the year, the resignation date shall be noted in the remarks column, and the actual attendance rate (%) shall be calculated based on the number of audit committee meetings during their tenure and their actual attendance.
2. Before the end of the fiscal year, if there are newly elected independent directors, both the new and the old independent directors should be listed, and the remarks column should indicate whether the independent director is newly elected, reelected, or retained, as well as the election date. The attendance rate (%) shall be calculated based on the number of meetings of the audit committee during their tenure and their actual attendance.

Does the company have other  important  in- formation that helps understand the operation of corporate govern- ance (including but not  limited  to  em- ployee benefits, employee  care,  in- vestor relations, supplier relation- ships, rights of stakeholders, status of education and training  for  direc- tors and supervi- sors, implementa- tion  of  risk  man- agement policies and  risk  measure- ment standards, ex- ecution of customer policies, and the company's pur- chase of liability insurance for direc- tors and supervi- sors)?    (I)  Employee benefits:  (1)  The  CEO  leads  management  executives  to  jointly  develop  organizational  needs  and  strategic objectives, so that colleagues can  clearly understand the company's future di-  rections and establish consensus among team  members.  (2) A company-wide communication conference is  held every six months to allow all employees  to  understand  business  operations,  quality  strategies, company strategy promotion, and  corporate culture development. Through two-  way communication, colleagues' opinions and  ideas are understood, and future execution is  used as a reference.  - (3) The company is based on the management phi- losophy  of  "integrity  sharing,  respect  for human nature" and treats employees with in- tegrity. Through annual education and train- ing plans for each level, a learning organi- zation is created, combined with employee ca- reer development, so that employees and the company can grow together.  - (4) The company has additional group insurance for  employees  to  improve  employee  protec- tion,  and  holds  labor-management  meetings and  occupational  safety  and  health  commit- tees every quarter to provide employees with transparent information and security rights.  - (II) Employee Care: Smooth communication channels are the main issue for employee care.  - (1) Project meetings: Through project-style dis- cussions, data analysis, and experience shar- ing, we enhance the establishment of mutual understanding  and  consensus,  and  improve problem-solving  abilities.  This  allows  for smoother cross-departmental communication and more diverse perspectives and views from employees.  - (2) Communication meetings with foreign labor: Bilingual personnel are assigned to each re- gion as communication bridges. They regularly care about the working and living conditions and needs of foreign laborers in Taiwan, and conduct opinion exchanges and communication.  No differ- ence found.   |

| (VII)  Implementation  of  Risk  Management  Policies  and  Risk Measurement Standards:  (1)  Risk  Management  Policies:  Control  potential  risks  within a tolerable level to achieve the goal of rational-  izing risk and return.  (2) Risk Measurement Standards: The company has estab-  lished  relevant  operational  guidelines  and  control  measures  to  establish  an  effective  risk  management  mechanism and evaluate and monitor its risk-bearing  capacity.  (3) Implementation of Business Operations: To effectively  identify, measure, monitor, and control various risks,  risk indicators are set, transactions are established, and  regular  assessments  are  conducted  to  achieve  a  risk  warning mechanism.  (4) Risk Issues and Management Framework:  Important risk items  Risk re-  view mech-  anism  Decision-making  and supervision.  Credit risk  Market risk  Liquidity risk  Operational risk.  Legal risk.  Supply chain risk.  High-risk and high-lev-  erage investment.  Management of lending  funds to others.  Management of endorse-  ment guarantee.  Operation management of  derivative trading.  Financial risks such as  interest rate fluctua-  tion, exchange rate  fluctuation, and infla-  tion.  Information security risk  management.  Special su-  pervisors re-  sponsible  for each  risk.  Board of Direc-  tors:  The highest de-  cision-making  unit for all  risk management  measures, re-  sponses, and im-  plementations.  Changes in the owner-  ship of directors, su-  pervisors, and major  shareholders.  Management of board of  directors and share-  holders' meetings.  Board of  Directors  Audit:  Supervision and  tracking of risk  issues   |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

| (5) Information security risk management policies and ex-  ecution:  The company's information security strategy focuses on three  aspects:  information  security  governance,  compli- ance with laws and regulations, and application of tech- nology, comprehensively improving the company's infor- mation security capabilities from systems to technology, from  personnel  to  organization.  In  view  of  the  current emerging trends in information security, such as DDoS at- tacks,  ransomware,  social  engineering  attacks,  phishing websites, etc., the company exchanges with international security vendors annually and regularly focuses on infor- mation security issues through project cooperation, con- ducts information system restoration and protection exer- cises, strengthens the emergency response capabilities of handling personnel, and aims to detect and complete the disposal in the first time.  The company has established "Software Management and Information Security Procedures," "Information Security Policy and Operational Procedures," and "Network Equip- ment Information Security Operational Procedures," etc., to conduct information security risk management and re- view the information security policy annually to enhance security management mechanisms. Precautionary measures for information security risks in 2022 include:  (1) Conducting annual company-wide information secu- rity  policy  promotion  and  regularly  announcing  infor- mation security protection messages.  (2) Regularly collecting firewall data, analyzing and for- mulating defense policies to ensure the company is not af- fected by external attacks.  (3) IT network firewall uses a four-in-one security protec- tion module.  (4) The anti-virus software uses the Apex One version to perform secondary filtering of malicious websites and ma- licious files, further reducing information security risks.  The report on the operation of information security man- agement in 2022 has been presented to the board of direc- tors on January 13, 2023. (V) Progress of sustainable development implementation and differences and reasons from sustainability practices guidelines for listed and OTC companies.

Implementation Status (Note 1)

Summary and Explanation

The company has established a sustainable development committee, which regularly reports to the chairman of the board and the board of directors on the implementation and tracking of sustainable management goals and policies.  The  sustainable  development  committee  has  three functional  groups,  namely  corporate  governance,  social responsibility,  and  environmental  protection,  which  are jointly responsible for reviewing the company's core operational capabilities with multiple mid- to high-level executives from different fields, and proposing and implementing sustainable development policies, systems, or related  management  guidelines  and  specific  promotion plans. The work plan for 2022 years has been submitted to the board of directors  on  November  2,  2022. The  directors suggest  that  the  management  team  should  continue  to strengthen  communication  with  investors,  evaluate  the cling wastewater, reducing annual production and usage of  water  by  1%,  and  conducting  more  than  2  public impact  of  the  zero-emission  initiative,  and  respond  accordingly. In 2023, the company will continue to set targets  for  reducing  electricity  consumption  by  5%,  recyspeeches per year. The progress of the targets will be reviewed quarterly.

Differences and reasons from sustainability practices guidelines for listed and OTC compa- nies.

No difference found.

Summary and Explanation

II.  Has  the  Com- pany conducted risk assessments on environmen- tal, social, and corporate govern- ance issues re- lated to its opera- tions,  in  accord- ance with the principles  of  ma- teriality,  and  es- tablished relevant risk  management policies or strate- gies? (Note 2)



Yes No  Summary and Explanation  2. The company continues to implement measures to save  electricity. In 2023, it plans to replace 181 sets of T8 flu-  orescent lamps, which is expected to save 5,430 kWh per  month. Additionally, two 150-ton ice water machines will  be added to replace the old 240-ton machine, which is es-  timated to save 20% of electricity, resulting in a total ex-  pected saving of 2,016 kWh per month.  3. The company produces surplus cores and recycled fin-  ished  products.  The  original  processing  method  was  to  outsource waste disposal through incineration. After be-  ing developed by technical personnel, the finished prod-  uct is shredded into powder after being peeled, and then  re-entered into the production line according to the pro-  portion of input. In 2022, 169 tons of waste cores were  reduced, resulting in a reduction of waste.  Inorganic sludge is recycled and reused instead of being  treated  as  waste. With  the  design  and  manufacture  of  a  drying machine by the company's technical development  personnel and vendors, the water content of the sludge is  reduced to below 1%, meeting the standard for recycling  and reuse. In addition to reducing outsourcing fees, this  also further achieves the goal of waste recycling. In 2022,  27 tons of sludge were reduced. In 2023, this plan will be  listed again, and a dedicated person will be responsible for  operating and optimizing the conditions, with the goal of  reducing 120 tons of sludge in one year.  5. During the production process of manufacturing golf  balls, recycling of the scraps generated from the ball cen-  ter, injection molding process, and the casing and ball cen-  ter of the defective product after dissection is carried out.  The scraps generated in the ball center process should be  recycled  and  used  in  the  ball  center  formulation.  The  scraps  generated  during  the  injection  molding  process   |

(II)

1. The Company has established management pro- cedures for salary, year-end bonus, performance bonus, incentive bonus, retirement system, em- ployee compensation, and employee stock owner- ship plan. These procedures are regularly re- viewed and revised each year based on factors such as labor market and economic environment. The Company participates in an annual market salary survey and adjusts overall compensation competitiveness based on market salary levels, economic trends, and individual performance. In 2022, the Company's average salary adjustment rate for both managerial and non-managerial positions in Taiwan was 4.7%. Regarding employee compensation, the Com- pany's bylaws stipulate that employee compensation shall be distributed based on the annual profit situation, not less than 1%, and director and supervisor compensation shall be distributed based on the annual profit situation not ex- ceeding 5%.

2.  The  Company  holds  regular  education  and training, practical exercises, and promotion of safety, emergency response, and code of conduct to  implement  a  performance  evaluation  system that enhances overall competitiveness. VII. Other important information to understand the execution of sustainable development:

(such as the systems, measures, and fulfillment status that Launch Technologies CO., LTD. has adopted for environmental protection, community involvement, social contributions, social services, public welfare, consumer rights, human rights, safety and health, and other social responsibilities activities)

- (I)Environmental Protection:
- (1) To address air pollution, the company has installed prevention facilities (zeolite concentration wheel and RTO system) for VOC adsorption, followed by over 90% effective treatment through the RTO system to reduce the impact of pollutants on the air.
- (2) Regarding the wastewater generated by the company, after treatment by its own wastewater treatment plant, 90% of it is reused within the factory (for flushing toilets, on-site processes, etc.), and adjustments have been made to the wastewater plant equipment and parameters to improve the efficiency and water quality of wastewater recovery and reduce the use of wastewater chemicals.
- (3) For the waste generated by the company, it is necessary to engage an approved waste removal and treatment unit recognized by the domestic environmental protection authorities, and to report and track the flow of waste. The legitimate waste disposal operators will properly handle the business waste to avoid environmental pollution.

## (V)Health and Safety:

- (1) Conduct biannual evaluations of the operational environment within the  plant  to  measure  employees'  exposure  to  hazards  such  as  dust, chemicals, and noise. Regular monitoring is conducted to detect abnormalities, and engineering improvement plans are proposed periodically to reduce potential hazards in the workplace.
- (2) Provide personal protective equipment for employees, such as earplugs, masks, and safety shoes. Additionally, a dedicated equipment improvement unit is responsible for researching energy-saving, labor-saving, and safety-enhancing improvements for the machinery.
- (3) Ban the use of carcinogenic substances, such as benzene and trichloroethylene, throughout the entire factory in accordance with EU regulations and foreign customer requirements.
- (4) Establish and implement biannual self-defense and fire safety plans in compliance with relevant laws and regulations, and report the results to local fire departments for record-keeping purposes.
- (5) Organize employee health promotion activities based on health examination results and colleague demand, planning a diverse range of activities to promote wellness. In cooperation with the National Defense Medical  Center's  802  Hospital,  conduct  annual  health  check-ups  and screenings for the four leading types of cancer, as well as work with local health centers to combat liver cancer.
- (6)  In  addition  to  the  four  major  workplace  plans  established  by  law (including maternal health protection, prevention of cerebrovascular and  cardiovascular  diseases,  human  hazard  assessment,  and  unlawful infringement),  establish  a  comprehensive  health  management  system based on health check-up results and health management. Analyze questionnaire items and results from previous years' health check-ups, and conduct  a  complete  health  control  mechanism  based  on  the  type  and severity  of  abnormalities,  including  classification,  tracking,  and medical consultation, to achieve effective, systematic, and sustained

- Note 4: If the issuance conditions of equity securities stipulate that unpaid dividends as of the end of the year should be accumulated and distributed in a year with earnings, the accumulated unpaid dividends up to the end of the year should be separately disclosed.
- Note 5: P/E ratio = Average closing price per share for the year / Earnings per share.
- Note 6: Payout ratio = Average closing price per share for the year / Cash dividend per share.
- Note 7: Dividend yield = Cash dividend per share / Average closing price per share for the year.

- Note 8: Net asset value per share and earnings per share should be filled in with the latest quarter data audited by the accountant as of the date of the annual report; other fields should be filled in with the data for the current year as of the date of the annual report.
- Note 9: Dividend distribution for the year 2021 has not been decided yet and will be determined after the annual shareholders' meeting.

## (VI) Company dividend policy and implementation status

- (1) Dividend policy:

This  company  adopts  a  surplus  dividend  policy  for  sustainable  operation  and  increased profits. If there is a surplus in the annual settlement, it shall be distributed in the following order:

1. Withholding income tax
2. Compensation for past years' losses.
3. 3. Allocation of 10% of the net profit to the statutory reserve until the reserve reaches the total amount of share capitalTen percent of net income is appropriated as legal reserve until the total amount equals the capital stock; the limit does not apply after the legal reserve equals the capital stock.
4. The special reserve should be allocated or reversed according to laws, regulations competent authority's stipulation.
5. The remaining balance after the above items have been allocated, plus the undistributed profits from the previous shall be accumulated as distributable profits to shareholders. The board of directors shall prepare a distribution proposal, which shall  be  submitted  to  the  shareholders'  meeting  for  approval.  The  distribution amount shall not be less than 50% of the distributable profits for the current year.

Taking into account the company's operating environment and growth prospects, future funding needs, and long-term financial planning, and to meet the shareholders' demand for cash, among the shareholder dividends distributed according to the preceding paragraph, the cash dividend shall not be less than 10% of the total dividend amount.

The company authorizes the board of directors, with the attendance of more than twothirds of the directors and the majority of the attending directors, to distribute all or part of the dividends, capital surplus, or statutory surplus reserve through the issuance of cash, and report to the shareholders' meeting. This provision does not apply to the requirement for approval by the shareholders' meeting.

## (II) Sector Overview

In 2022, as the COVID-19 pandemic is alleviated, major golf-prevalent countries have gradually relaxed their restrictions. The impact on golf is still positive, and the annual growth of the major golf consumer market in 2022 is still over 5%. The golf boom caused by COVID-19 continues, as it is one of the best leisure sports and social activities. In addition, the continued impact of economic stimulus plans in various countries, and the strong economic performance of the United States as a major golf consumer country, will promote strong growth in golf in 2022.

Golf is still most popular in the United States, Japan, and Europe, while it is gradually becoming popular in Asia due to the vigorous economic development, making it the fastest-growing region. According to estimates from golf sports information in the United States and Japan, the current global golf population is approximately 66 million, of which the United States has the highest number with about 25 million, followed by Japan with about 10 million. In recent years, there has been a significant growth in the golf population in Asia and emerging countries, especially in Korea which has experienced the most rapid growth. Japan's well-known professional player winning the U.S. Golf Masters and the stimulation from the 2020 Tokyo Olympics have also contributed to the positive outlook of the market.

Golf sports and economic development are closely related. In the era of global economic growth, commercial activities have dramatically increased, new golf courses are being developed rapidly, the middle class population is growing, and other factors provide a favorable environment for the sport of golf. The establishment and promotion of international events have further promoted the popularization of golf sports. In addition to the four major annual tournaments, professional tournaments such as the PGA Tour, European Tour, and Asian Tour are held in different regions, creating opportunities for

players of different nationalities to compete together and promoting the internationalization of regional competitions. This has stimulated an increase in the number of people participating in golf sports and pushed for the popularization of golf. In recent years, the increase in the number of golf courses has provided convenient conditions for more people to participate in the sport, gradually making the small white ball that was once considered an aristocratic sport more accessible to the public. In addition to the middle class viewing it as the best channel for business socializing, new families also view it as a sport for fitness and socializing, leading to the booming development of the golf market year by year. Especially after 2013, the number of professional golf events in Asia has increased, and both the PGA Tour and the European Tour have increased the number of tournaments in Asia, which has greatly helped promote golf in the Asian market.

In  terms  of  the  analysis  of  the  golf  sport  product  market,  the  market  and  product trends of golf sport are currently dominated by major brand manufacturers. For golf balls in particular, the leaders are American companies such as Titleist, Callaway, TaylorMade, and Japanese companies such as Bridgestone, Srixon, Mizuno, etc. This is due to factors such as the major brand manufacturers having abundant capital to play roles in  marketing, promotion, and creating new products, and their ability to shape their image through well-known sports stars to attract sports enthusiasts to follow their brand. This has further enabled major brand products to hold an absolute advantage in the market,  especially  with  American  brands  such  as  Titleist,  Callaway,  TaylorMade, Bridgestone, and Srixon, which together account for over 80% of global sales of golf balls. The operating strategies of major brand manufacturers often focus on marketing, research and development, and other businesses, while outsourcing the manufacturing process to other production manufacturers. Taiwanese manufacturers have the best manufacturing capabilities and the largest production capacity for golf equipment globally. On the other hand, they also focus on the rise of new markets in Asia and global deployment. Well-known brands such as Callaway, TaylorMade, Bridgestone, Mizuno, and Wilson have all entrusted Taiwanese manufacturers to produce their golf balls. As a result, Taiwan has become an important global production base for golf balls.

The manufacturing technology and construction of golf balls have undergone significant changes along with product improvements. Before the 1990s, golf balls were mostly constructed with a rubber-wound core, with performance and distance being less than ideal due to material and structural factors. After the 1990s, golf balls gradually shifted towards solid rubber cores and outer shell materials improved, resulting in a significant increase in distance. In the past decade, multi-layered golf balls with complex structures and outer shell layers coated with resin have been developed. Manufacturing technology has become more sophisticated and complex, requiring precision instruments and quality control based on laboratory physics data. Product design has also become a competitive advantage among major manufacturers. Every aspect of golf ball construction, such as core structure, layering, shell hardness, material composition, and dimple structure, is divided into highly precise research and development factors, making golf balls a highly technological product. Due to the increased difficulty of design, major brands have applied for patents to protect their research and development results, raising the entry barrier and making the market a battlefield for only a few international giants.

In terms of the global business model for the golf industry, apart from a few brands that specialize in golf ball production, most brands adopt a composite business model, with the brand as a backing and a diverse range of golf products, including clubs, balls, equipment, and even clothing. Many of these brands have outsourced the production of

clubs and other products to Asian manufacturers, especially Taiwanese contract manufacturers. With advanced manufacturing technology, excellent development and design capabilities, skilled human resources, and sufficient capital capacity, Taiwanese manufacturers have captured more than 90% of the contract manufacturing market in the golf ball production field. This has deepened the dependence of major brands on Taiwanese manufacturers, and the marketing-design-manufacturing division of labor has become more distinct, forming an inseparable partnership. In recent years, major brand manufacturers have actively entered the field of golf and continue to adopt this successful model. Therefore, Taiwanese manufacturers will also play the role of best manufacturing and development, and cost control in the golf market, following the development trajectory of golf equipment to create another important value-added product.

dustry (2) Various product development trends

1. Golf balls are products with complex physical characteristics and diverse product types. Differences in materials, structures, sizes, weights, and shell designs will create differences in the performance and characteristics of golf balls, and even subtle differences will have clever effects on distance, spin, and wind resistance during a swing, thus affecting the results of the competition. In the early days of golf, the golf ball core was made of liquid material and wrapped with winding coils. Today, the structure is made of rubber and resin for the inner and outer layers respectively, and the designed physical properties are also calculated with precision data. As golf has developed, its product specifications have been standardized. The global standard for golf ball specifications is set by the United States Golf Association (USGA), with a minimum diameter of 42.67mm and a maximum weight of 45.92g. The initial velocity of the ball is also limited under specific conditions.
2. In addition to standard specifications, golf balls also have different structures and materials depending on the research and development of the manufacturers. Generally, when choosing a golf ball, market users distinguish between different types based on the ball's structure. They can be divided into the following categories:

A.One-Piece-Ball

A one-piece ball, also known as a solid ball, is made by molding a hard rubber core and then printing and painting it for protection. Due to its durability, it is mostly used for practice balls.

B.Two-Piece-Ball

A two-piece ball consists of a large rubber core and a relatively thin outer shell. Because of its excellent performance and moderate price, it is the most common golf ball design. The outer shell of the ball is made of a special plastic material.

C. Three-Piece-Ball

It consists of a rubber or plastic core and a middle layer, with

an outer shell made of a special plastic material or PU material to simultaneously meet the distance requirements of long clubs and the control of spin performance required for short clubs. Currently, most balls with the highest spin and the best hitting feel still use this proven three-layer structure.

## D.Multi-layered Golf Ball

The design of multi-layered golf ball aims to produce optimal results under any level of swing force. The ball core is designed to achieve maximum distance off the tee, while the intermediate layer is suitable for high force iron shots, and the outer shell is optimized for optimal ball feel, as well as spin control during half swings, chips, and putts.

In terms of usage, one-piece balls are generally only used on practice ranges, while two-piece balls are softer, spin faster and fly farther, and are cheaper, making them popular with casual consumers. Three-layer and four-layer balls require high levels of swing skill to fully utilize their performance. As they are versatile and easy to control, they are favored by high-level golfers. Although the structure of golf balls has become increasingly complex with innovations by manufacturers, all types of golf balls on the market have their own uses and target audiences, and are developed concurrently rather than mutually exclusive.

3. Due to the advancement in rubber formulation and process technology in recent years, we have been able to create products with a soft feel and great distance, which provides significant benefits for those pursuing distance and forgiveness in golf. Soft overall feel is a trend in current golf product development,  as  many  products  in  the  market  are  designed  with  this feature.
- (3) Competitive landscape:

The terminal consumer market for golf is dominated by major brands from the US and Japan. The golf market has a brand worshiping mentality, and as a result, competition is dominated by a few large manufacturers. Their annual marketing expenses, advertising spending, and channel construction budgets are staggering. Especially since the golf industry is a global market, with enormous economic scale, only a few financially strong companies are able to manage their brand. Moreover, the high barriers created by the protection of various patents make the market competition extremely difficult. Therefore, only those who have worked hard for a long time and have established their brand recognition can break through. Therefore, in the golf industry, there is a clear division of labor between the marketing and manufacturing sides, with each party specializing in its own value chain. After more than 30 years of development, Taiwan's golf industry has become a global production center and a major OEM and ODM partner for international companies. In recent years, golf equipment manufacturers have actively invested in the OEM manufacturing of golf products in response to the downstream customers' diversified business strategies. With the efforts of the R&amp;D team,  our  company  continues  to  develop  high-value-added  products  to  strengthen

## market competitiveness.

More than 85% of the golf market is dominated by major brand products. Consumers have a brand worship mentality, and major brands not only rely on marketing and operations but also outsource their production lines to manufacturers with high technical expertise and experience who can meet their production capacity needs, especially in the Asian region where outsourcing is prevalent. In recent years, the golf industry has been impacted by the financial crisis, and the market size has not expanded. In 2022, the global total sales exceeded 90 million units, and in terms of shipments, the company's shipments accounted for nearly 20% of the global golf ball market. As the Asian market is the fastest-growing economy, and the trend of international brands outsourcing to manufacturers has already taken root, Taiwan's golf ball manufacturers have established a close partnership with major brands in the outsourcing industry. Therefore, it is expected that the future orders for golf ball manufacturing will also have strong growth potential.

- (3) Future market supply and demand conditions and growth prospects:
1. Future supply and demand conditions:

The golf market is mainly dominated by the United States, Europe, and Japan, which account for over 90% of the entire market demand.

Since the beginning of 2020, changes in people's lifestyles due to COVID-19, coupled with large-scale economic stimulus plans in various countries, have made golf a safe sport and social activity during the pandemic. Strong demand has been seen since the second half of 2020, and demand is expected to continue to grow until 2022.

In terms of supply, the golf ball market is still dominated by well-known American and Japanese brands, including Callaway, TaylorMade, Titleist, Bridgestone, Mizuno,  and  Srixon.  In  competitions  and  business  occasions,  higher-quality  golf balls are more popular, while lower-quality golf balls still have their market in general practice ranges, family gatherings, and other occasions. Therefore, non-

Unit: NT$ 1,000

well-known brand manufacturers supply this market through low-price strategies. Well-known  brand  manufacturers  continuously  design,  improve,  and  innovate their golf balls, and consumers also have a mentality to follow trends, making products have a certain life cycle. On the other hand, well-known brand manufacturers also try to lower production costs and outsource production lines to Asia to expand their market downwards. In addition to some well-known brand manufacturers that still have their own production lines, outsourcing production capacity has become a trend, and Asian suppliers now play an important role. Due to the increasingly complex production technology and the involvement of patent protection, except for low-end products and expired patent golf balls, producers are still constrained by the major brand manufacturers, forming a barrier to entry and limiting the market supply to a certain extent.

In terms of demand, in recent years, golf tournament television broadcasts have become more frequent, and many new-generation famous golf stars have emerged successively, further promoting the vigorous development of golf. The number of golf players outside of the United States and Japan has rapidly increased. With the popularization of golf, the structure of the golf population has also changed. Golf has gradually become a sport for the middle class from an exclusive sport for highincome people. The age group is rapidly expanding downward, and the female sports population is growing rapidly. The trend shows that more and more young and female consumers are participating in golf. The 2020 Olympics were held in Tokyo, and Asian-American athlete Xander Schauffele won the gold medal. Our country's top player Pan Cheng-tsung won the silver medal, directly stimulating the growth and development of the Asian golf industry. It is expected that the global golf market will be further boosted as a result.

2. Future growth potential.
- A. Increase and Popularization of Sports Population The impact of COVID-19 will persist for several years, and during this period,  the  population  of golf continues to grow. In addition, golf is a lowimpact sport that is not limited by age and has social characteristics. With the lush greenery of the course, it is close to nature, with fresh air and sunshine, and is full of opportunities for interpersonal friendship and exchange. For modern people pursuing health, integration of relaxation, and broadening of the mind, it has positive value and has become one of the mainstream sports, deeply yearned for by those who pursue high-quality leisure life. With the global economic development and the increase of average income, this originally high-income aristocratic sport became popular in many countries after World War II, and the golf population quickly spread to the general income level. In recent years, frequent television broadcasts of golf tournaments have nurtured many new generation and well-known golf stars, further driving the vigorous development of golf and increasing the number of golf players outside the United States and Japan. With the popularity of golf, the structure of golf players has changed. Golf is no longer exclusively for the wealthy, but gradually spreading to middle-income families; the age group is expanding downwards, and the proportion of female players is increasing.
- B. The Asian market has great growth potential.

As the US market gradually matures, golf businesses have been expanding overseas. The proportion of sales in overseas markets has been increasing year by year, especially in the Asian market, which is a battleground for all.

In recent years, with large-scale foreign investment, the construction of golf courses in India and Southeast Asia is expected to increase steadily every year. The promotion of golf and the internationalization of tournaments have accelerated, and the per capita income has greatly increased. The potential of the golf market is considerable.

- C. Trend of repeat consumption Golf is an indispensable product in the sport of golf and is a consumable in golf sports. Therefore, consumers will clearly purchase multiple sets of golf balls when buying golf equipment, which will drive the trend of repeat consumption of golf balls and maintain healthy growth of the entire industry.

## (4) Competitive advantage:

1. New product and technology development capabilities Our company collaborates with institutions such as the Metal Industries Research &amp; Development Centre, Industrial Technology Research Institute, and academic units of universities for the long-term development of new materials and technology for golf balls. We develop new products and technologies, combine fast response to market demand and high-performance products, cooperate with customers, explore new product markets and golf ball products with special specifications, create demand and expand market share.
2. Short product development cycle, rapidly provide customer and market demand. Due to intense competition and rapid market changes, timely response to market demand is the key to the success of the company and products. Product life cycle, development, and production time have been significantly reduced. The company must develop and mass-produce new products at the fastest possible speed to meet customer demand. We have established long-term partnerships with institutions such as the Industrial Technology Research Institute to research and develop new golf materials and technologies that respond quickly to market demands and provide high-performance products that meet the needs of our customers.
3. We offer customers a one-stop shopping service. Our company is a professional golf ball manufacturer, offering a wide range of products from single-layer practice balls to high-end multi-layer tournament balls. Our products are diverse and complete, and our quality and performance are on par with leading brands in the market. We provide OEM/ODM customers with one-stop procurement services, eliminating the need to place orders with multiple manufacturers simultaneously. In addition, we can assist our customers with golf ball product marketing information to facilitate product promotion.
4. We are also constructing an information network system to enhance our competitive advantage.

Due to the important role of information technology (IT) in today's competitive business  environment,  our  company  has  implemented  an  Enterprise  Resource Planning (ERP) system to achieve digital transformation in management and improve the efficiency of resource utilization. Customers can also check their order status in real-time. Furthermore, we have introduced a Manufacturing Execution System (MES) to monitor real-time information on raw materials and machine operations. This enables us to adjust inventory and machine utilization in a timely manner, improving our production capacity and accelerating the time to market for new products. We also strive to establish close collaboration with our upstream and downstream partners and customers to enhance our competitive advantage.

- (5) Positive factors, negative factors, and corresponding strategies for our company's development vision:
1. Positive factors
- K. The proportion of OEM manufacturing by American companies is expected to increase in the future.   Domestic manufacturers in the golf industry have accumulated more than 30 years of experience in OEM manufacturing, making them second only to Japan and the United States in terms of product design capabilities. In addition, domestic manufacturers have a competitive edge in terms of quality, development efficiency, and production capacity, thanks to their strategic partnerships with major brand companies and relatively lower costs compared to other OEM manufacturers. As competition in the golf market intensifies, major American manufacturers that used to dominate the market have transferred their OEM opportunities to Asian factories. This has led to a rapid growth in our company's OEM orders, and the trend of Asian OEM manufacturing is expected to continue, providing our company with huge growth potential in the OEM market.
- L. Impact of COVID-19 COVID-19 has led people to reduce travel and change their work patterns, greatly increasing their leisure time at home, and resulting in a significant increase in the number of people participating in socially distant activities such as golf.
- M. Golf sports are gaining popularity.  With economic development, the demand for leisure activities is increasing in various countries. Golf sports have grown year by year in Asia and emerging countries due to media coverage and social trends. The number of golf courses being built is also growing rapidly, providing more space for those who enjoy this sport and attracting more people to participate in it. This has driven the development of the golf equipment market. Looking to the future, the use of new golf designs and materials will further stimulate market demand in line with the growth of the golf population.
- N. Focus on research and development, rich experience in production.

The Launch Technologies CO., LTD. has accumulated years of manufacturing experience, continuously improved production technology, and collaborated with metal industry research and development centers, industrial research institutes, and academic units of universities to develop material formulas, improve product quality, shorten process time, and reduce manufacturing costs. Its product quality is stable and deeply trusted by customers. The company invests heavily in research and development to reduce customer design and development costs, thereby enhancing its competitiveness.

2. Adverse factors and response strategies.
- A. High proportion of exports, greatly affected by exchange rate fluctuations. The majority of Launch Technologies CO., LTD.'s products are exported, and exchange rate fluctuations have a significant impact on the acceptance of orders and profitability. Corresponding strategies:

Maintain close contact with our partner banks to monitor the trend of exchange rates and utilize appropriate foreign exchange hedging tools to mitigate the risk of exchange rate fluctuations, or negotiate price adjustments with customers to respond promptly to the impact of exchange rate fluctuations.

- B. The threshold for brand management is not easy to cross, and our orders are mainly based on OEM.

Due to the phenomenon of the survival of the fittest in the sports goods industry and the misconception that consumers prefer branded products, even though the quality of our golf products has been recognized by the domestic leading manufacturers, we still cannot overcome the competitive advantage of internationally renowned brands, so OEM orders remain our main source of orders.

Corresponding strategies:

By enhancing R&amp;D capabilities and strengthening product design ability, we aim to improve product quality, enhance marketing capabilities, and conduct research and development on new products. We will quickly inform customers of our R&amp;D achievements to seek opportunities for producing high valueadded products and to enhance profitability. In the Asian and Japanese markets,  we will develop ODM product businesses with channel partners for direct sales and establish long-term sales strategies for our own brands.

- C. Order restrictions from international major factories increase production difficulty.

Some of the materials and suppliers for the products in the international major factory orders that the company undertakes are directly specified by the customers.  Furthermore,  the  delivery  requirements  are  becoming  increasingly shorter, which increases the difficulty of production progress and cost control.

Corresponding strategies:

By enhancing R&amp;D capability and product design, improving product quality,  strengthening  marketing  capability,  and  developing  new  products,  we will quickly inform customers of our R&amp;D achievements to compete for high value-added product opportunities and improve profit margin. In the Asian and Japanese markets, we will develop a long-term sales strategy for ODM products  sold  directly  to  distribution  channels  and  establishing  our  own brand.

- (II)The restrictions on orders from international manufacturers have increased the difficulty of production.
- (1) Important uses of the product:

Golf is a part of the golf sports industry and serves as a subject for players to demonstrate their swing control ability and skills. In golf tournaments, players formulate a swing strategy by considering factors such as the course environment, weather conditions, and distance to

- the  hole  in  order  to  hit  the  ball  into  the  green  with  the  lowest possible number of swings. Therefore, the material characteristics of the selected golf ball are highly valued, including factors such as ball speed trajectory, flight distance, and rolling ability. Manufacturers also constantly strive to develop golf balls suitable for various swing conditions.
- (2) Currently, the trend of golf sports is becoming younger, more civilian, popular, and leisure-oriented. In addition to its athletic function, golf also represents social status, making the consumer market for golf balls need to strive towards designing products that cater to various levels of demand. Therefore, in the direction of product development and research, there are also different design requirements for high-end, sophisticated, functional, leisure, and popular demands.

## IV. Environmental expenditure information

|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

Estimated amounts and corresponding measures that may occur currently or in the future

Measures taken (including improvement measures):

Our company belongs to the sporting goods manufacturing industry, mainly producing golf balls. We have passed the regular audits of ISO14001:2015 and ISO45001:2018 ARES certification, and our certificates remain valid. We are committed to preventing safety and health hazards for personnel in the manufacturing process and reducing the environmental impact during the manufacturing process. Our company's main environmental prevention measures are as follows:

- (1) To  address  noise  pollution,  engineering  controls  and  scheduling adjustments are implemented based on regional monitoring results to improve control methods.
- (2) Equipment improvements and process management are implemented to improve the work environment for powder blending operators and enhance environmental controls.
- (3) Energy-saving and carbon-reducing measures include daily air conditioning temperature and usage time settings, energy-saving controls in public areas, and assessment and replacement of old, energy-consuming equipment.
- (4) Water and waste management measures include controlling the use efficiency of water  in  production  equipment,  treating  and  recycling  wastewater,  adjusting wastewater treatment equipment and parameters to improve wastewater recycling

efficiency and water quality, reducing the use of chemicals, and controlling production quality to effectively utilize water resources and reduce waste.

- (5) Non-compliance with air pollution emission standards due to odor sensory detection: In response to the total amount control in the Kaohsiung-Pingtung area, we have enhanced the efficiency of prevention equipment for fixed pollution sources and strengthened patrols to ensure proper operation of prevention equipment. We adjust production capacity according to reports of poor air quality and regularly self-inspect perimeter odor.
- (6) Continuously maintain the operation of zeolite rotary wheel and RTO systems to ensure compliance with legal operations.

## VI. Information Security Management

- (I) Information Security Risk Management Framework, Information Security Policy,

Specific Management Plan, and Resources Invested in Information Security Management:

- (1)  Information Security Risk Management Framework

Our company established the "Enterprise Information Security Organization" in 2019 to formulate company information security policies, plan, execute, promote, and implement under the information department of the management division, in order to reduce information security risks and enhance colleagues' awareness of good information security practices. We regularly report on the status of information security governance to the board of directors.

<!-- image -->

Corporate Information Security Governance Organization Framework.

## (2) Information Security Policy

To effectively implement information security management, the Enterprise Information Security Organization holds regular monthly meetings to review abnormal situations, propose corresponding measures to handle and improve them, and review the applicability and protective measures of information security policies, making necessary revisions.

The company's information security strategy focuses on three aspects: information security governance, compliance with laws and regulations, and application of technology, comprehensively improving the company's information security capabilities from systems to technology, from personnel to organization. In view of the current emerging trends in information security, such as DDoS attacks, ransomware, social engineering attacks, phishing websites,  etc.,  the  company  exchanges  with  international  security  vendors  annually  and regularly focuses on information security issues through project cooperation, conducts information system restoration and protection exercises, strengthens the emergency response capabilities of handling personnel, and aims to detect and complete the disposal in the first time. In order to strengthen information security management, internal policy implementation is enforced, and a rigorous network control mechanism is established to create various types of detection systems, in order to detect problems early and perform repair procedures.

## (3) Specific Management Plan:

Internal:

▲ Information Security Policy: Review and revise the information security policy every six months as appropriate.

- ▲ Personnel Awareness: Conduct regular information security policy education for employees every year and announce security information irregularly.
- ▲ Computer Equipment Security: Install anti-virus software on computers, conduct regular antivirus checks, and make appropriate corrections to execution strategies.

▲ Application Security: Arrange an annual disaster recovery drill plan to ensure the restorability of important systems.

External:

▲ Network Security: Cooperate with telecommunications service providers to monitor information security vulnerabilities and enhance external network protection.

- ▲ Abnormal Data Monitoring: Regularly collect firewall data, analyze and develop defense policies to ensure that the company is not subject to external attacks.

- (2) If there have been any changes in the company's auditors in the past five years, the reasons for the change of the former and successor auditors should be explained as follows:

In 2008, our auditor was PwC Taiwan United Accounting Firm. From 2009 to 2011, we changed to Ernst &amp; Young United Accounting Firm to increase our consultation  channels  for  accounting  expertise  and  establish  more  diversified service resources. Starting from 2012, in order to coordinate with the group's operational management needs, we adopted the same auditor team as our parent company. However, both PwC Taiwan United Accounting Firm and Ernst &amp; Young United Accounting Firm are among the top four accounting firms in Taiwan and have comparable audit quality. In the fourth quarter of 2013, PwC Taiwan United Accounting Firm underwent internal organizational adjustments and was replaced by the auditors Wang Guohua and Lin Ziyu. In the  first quarter of 2016, PwC Taiwan United Accounting Firm underwent internal organizational adjustments and was replaced by the auditors Wang Guohua and Liao A-Shen. In the  first quarter of 2016, PwC Taiwan United Accounting Firm underwent

internal organizational adjustments and was replaced by the auditors Wu Chienchih and Liao A-Shen.  In the  second quarter of 2022, PwC Taiwan United Accounting Firm underwent internal organizational adjustments and was replaced by the auditors Wu Chien-chih and Wang Chun-Kai.These changes had little impact on the company's finances.

Describe the reasons for the changes in financial ratios of the recent two years (increase or decrease of less than 20% is exempted)

1. The current ratio increased, mainly due to an increase in bank deposits and inventory.
2. The quick ratio increased, mainly due to an increase in current bank deposits and inventory.
3. The interest coverage ratio increased, mainly due to an increase in pre-tax profit in the current period.
4. The turnover ratios of fixed assets, plant and equipment, and total assets increased, mainly due to an increase in net sales in the current period.
5. Various financial ratios for profitability increased, mainly due to an increase in profit in the current period.
6. Various financial ratios for cash flow increased, mainly due to an increase in net income in the current period, which led to an increase in net cash flow from operating activities.
7. Operating leverage decreased, mainly due to the increase in operating income in the current period.

Note 1: Financial data for the years 2018-2022 have been audited and certified by the accountant.

Note 2: The formulas for financial analysis calculations are as follows:

1. Financial Structure
- (1) Debt to asset ratio = Total liabilities / Total assets.
- (2) Ratio of long-term funds to net property, plant, and equipment = (Total equity+ Non-current liabilities) / Net property, plant, and equipment.
2. Solvency
- (1) Current ratio = Current assets / Current liabilities.
- (2) Quick ratio = (Current assets - Inventory - Prepaid expenses) / Current liabilities.
- (3) Interest Coverage Ratio = Net Profit Before Income Tax and Interest Expense / Current Interest Expenditure
3. Operating Performance
- (1) Turnover ratio of accounts receivable (including accounts receivable and notes receivable arising from sales) = Net sales / Average balance of accounts receivable (including accounts receivable and notes receivable arising from sales).
- (2) Average collection period = 365 / Turnover ratio of accounts receivable.
- (3) Inventory turnover ratio = Cost of goods sold / Average inventory.
- (4) Turnover ratio of accounts payable (including accounts payable and notes payable arising from purchases) = Cost of goods sold / Average balance of accounts payable (including accounts payable and notes payable arising from

purchases).

- (5) Average sales period = 365 / Inventory turnover ratio.
- (6) Turnover ratio of property, plant and equipment = Net sales / Average net property, plant and equipment.
- (7) Total asset turnover ratio = Net sales / Average total assets.

4. Profitability

- (1) Return on assets = (Net income after tax + Interest expense × (1 - Tax rate)) / Average total assets.
- (2) Return on Equity = Post-Tax Profit or Loss / Average Total Equity.
- (3) Profit Margin = Post-Tax Profit or Loss / Net Sales.
- (4) Earnings Per Share = (Gain or Loss Attributable to Owners of the Parent Company - Preference Dividend) / Weighted Average Number of Issued Shares.
5. Cash Flow
- (1) Cash Ratio = Operating Cash Flow / Current Liabilities.
- (2) Net Cash Flow Coverage Ratio = Average Operating Cash Flow of the Latest 5 Years / (Capital Expenditure + Increase in Inventory + Cash Dividends of the Latest 5 Years).
- (3) Cash Reinvestment Ratio = (Operating Cash Flow - Cash Dividends) / (Gross Fixed Assets + Long-term Investments + Other Non-current Assets + Working Capital)
6. Degree of Leverage ：
- (1) Degree of Operating Leverage = (Net Operating Income - Variable Operating Costs and Expenses) / Operating Profit
- (2) Degree of Financial Leverage = Operating Profit / (Operating Profit - Interest Expense).
