Key audit matters are those matters that, in our  professional judgment, were of most significance in our audit of Launch Corporation's financial statements for the year ended December 31, 2022.

## Decrease in accounts receivable

## Explanation

Please refer to Note 4(8) and (9) in the financial statements for t he accounting policy regarding accounts receivable and impairment assessment. Please refer to Note 5 for the uncertainty of accounting estimates and assumptions regarding accounts receivable. Please refer to Note 6(4) in the financial statements for an explanation of the net amount of accounts receivable.

Launch Technologies CO., LTD. must exercise judgment to identify the factors t h a t   a ff e c t   t h e   f u t u r e   r e c o v e r a b i l i t y   o f   a c c o u n t s   r e c e i v a b l e   a n d   c o n s i d e r   r e a s o n a b l e and supportable information regarding the time value of money and future economic conditions when measuring expected credit losses. Management's judgments are based on relevant supporting documents. Therefore, the auditor considers the evaluation of the impairment of accounts receivable of Launch Technologies CO., LTD. to be one of the most important audit matters.

## Corresponding audit program

In response to the above key audit matters, the auditor has performed the followi n g   m ai n procedures:

1. Based on our understanding of the operating and sales t ransactions with count e r p a r t i e s   o f   L a u n c h  Te c h n o l o g i e s   C O . ,   LT D . ,   w e   a s s e s s e d   t h e   r e a s o n a b l e n e s s   o f t h e   a l l o w a n c e   f o r   d o u b t f u l   a c c o u n t s   a n d   t h e   a d e q u a c y   o f   t h e   p o l i c i e s   a n d   p r o c e dures adopted, including the objective evidence on which the loss rate was based, such as customer characteristics, evaluation of historical collection experience and future economic conditions, and compared the allowance for doubtful accounts policies applied during the financial reporting period for consistency.
2. Evaluate the reasonableness of the expected loss rates provided by management

for each overdue period, supported by relevant supporting documents.

3. We verified the correctness of the ageing of the accounts receivable to confirm t h a t   t h e   r e p o r t   i n f o r m a t i o n   i s   c o n s i s t e n t   w i t h   i t s   p o l i c i e s .
4. After conducting substantive procedures on significant accounts receivable, we evaluated the likelihood of collection for such accounts through receipt testing.

## Inventory evaluation

## Explanation

For the accounting policy of inventory valuation, please refer to Note 4(11) of the financial statements; for the uncertainty of accounting estimates and assumptions related to inventory valuation, please refer to Note 5 of the financial statements. Explanation of Inventory Accounting, please refer to Note 6(5) of the financial statements.

Launch Technologies CO., LTD. manufactures and sells golf balls. Due to the growth of the golf ball market being influenced by fluctuations in the international economy, there may be situations where inventory may become obsolete or suffer from decline in value. The inventory of Launch Technologies CO., LTD. is measured at the lower of cost or net realizable value. For inventory with a certain age or for those identified as obsolete, the cost will be reduced to net realizable value. As the evaluation process may consider the judgment of the management based on relevant supporting documents, it is an area that requires judgment during the audit. Considering that the inventory of Launch Technologies CO., LTD. and its provision for decline in value losses have a significant impact on the financial statements, the auditor considers the inventory valuation of Launch Technologies CO., LTD. as one of the most important matters to be audited for the c urrent year.

## Corresponding audit program

In response to the above key audit matters, the auditor has performed the followi n g   m ai n procedures:

- 1.It is reasonable to use the consistent policy of assessing the loss of allowance to reduce inventory to market during the period of comparing the financial statements and to assess the policy proposed.
- Understand the inventory management process, review the annual inventory plan, and participate in the annual inventory count to evaluate the effectiveness of

management's judgment and control over obsolete inventory.

Conduct sample tests on individual inventory items to assess the reasonableness of t h e   p r o v i s i o n   f o r   i n v e n t o r y   i m p a i r m e n t   l o s s   b a s e d   o n   t h e i r   n e t   r e a l i z a b l e   v a l u e .

## The responsibility for the financial statements lies with the management and the governance units.

The responsibility of management is to prepare financial statements in accordance with the Financial Reporting Standards for Securities Issuers and the I n t e r n a t i o n a l   F i n a n c i a l   R e p o r t i n g   S t a n d a r d s ,   I n t e r n a t i o n a l  A c c o u n t i n g   S t a n d ards, Interpretations, and Interpretive Bulletins approved and published by the Financial Supervisory Commission, and to maintain necessary internal controls r e l a t e d   t o   f i n a n c i a l   s t a t e m e n t   p r e p a r a t i o n   t o   e n s u r e   t h a t   t h e   f i n a n c i a l   s t a t e ments are not materially misstated due to fraud or error.

I n   p r e p a r i n g   t h e   f i n a n c i a l   s t a t e m e n t s ,   t h e   r e s p o n s i b i l i t y   o f   m a n a g e m e n t also includes evaluating Launch Technologies CO., LTD.'s ability to continue as a going concern, disclosing relevant m atters, and using a going concern bas i s   o f   a c c o u n t i n g ,   u n l e s s   m a n a g e m e n t   i n t e n d s   t o   l i q u i d a t e   L a u n c h   Te c h n o l o gies CO., LTD. or cease operations, or no other realistic alternative exists other than liquidation or cessation of operations.

The governance unit (including the audit committee) of Launch Technologies CO., LTD. is responsible for overseeing the financial reporting process.

## Account's responsibilities for the audit of parent company only financial statements

The objectives of accounts for auditing parent company only financial s t a t e m e n t s   a r e   t o   o b t a i n   r e a s o n a b l e   a s s u r a n c e   a b o u t   w h e t h e r   t h e   p a r e n t   c o m pany only financial statements as a whole are free from any material misstatement due to fraud or error and to issue an accountant ' s   r e p o r t   a c c o r d i n g l y.   I t i s   r e a s o n a b l e   a s s u r a n c e ,   w h i c h   i s   a   h i g h   l e v e l   o f   a s s u r a n c e ,   b u t   t h e   a u d i t   w o r k performed in accordance with the auditing standards of the Republic of China cannot guarantee the detection of material misstatement s in the individual financial statements. Misstatements may arise from error or fraud. A misstated dollar amount, individually or in the aggregate, that could be reasonable predicted to influence the economic decision of the user of the parent company only financial statements can be viewed as material.

Our auditor exercised professional judgment and skepticism in accordance with the auditing standards of the Republic of China. We also performed the f o l l o w i n g   t a s k s :

1. We identified and assessed the risks of material misstatement of the parent company only financial statements, whether due to fraud or errors, designed and performed audit procedures according to those risks, and obtained audit evidence that can sufficiently and appropriately form the basis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from fraud is higher than for the one resulting from error because fraud may involve collusion, forgery, i n t e n t i o n a l   o m i s s i o n s ,   m i s r e p r e s e n t a t i o n s ,   o r   t h e   o v e r r i d e   o f   i n t e r n a l   c o n t r o l .
2. Obtain the necessary understanding of internal controls related to the audit and design appropriate audit procedures for the circumstances, but the purpose is not t o   e x p r e s s   a n   o p i n i o n   o n   t h e   e ff e c t i v e n e s s   o f   L a u n c h  Te c h n o l o g i e s   C O . ,   LT D . ' s i n t e r n a l   c o n t r o l s .
3. We evaluated the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates and related disclosures made by management.
4. Based on the audit evidence obtained, we draw a conclusion on the appropriateness of management's use of the going concern basis of accounting and whether there i s   a   s i g n i f i c a n t   u n c e r t a i n t y   t h a t   m a y   c a s t   s i g n i f i c a n t   d o u b t   o n   L a u n c h  Te c h n o l o gies CO., LTD.'s ability to continue as a going concern. If we beli eve that such significant uncertainties exist, the auditor shall alert the financial statement users in the audit report to pay attention to the related disclosures in the financial

statements, or revise the audit opinion if such disclosures are inappropri ate. Our conclusions are based on the audit evidence obtained up to the date of this accountant ' s report. However, future events or circumstances may arise that could cause Launch Technologies CO., LTD. to no longer have the ability to continue as a going concern.

5. We evaluated the overall presentation, structure and contents of the parent company only financial statements, including the attached notes, and whether the parent company on financial statements represent the underlying  transactions and events in a fair manner.

## 四、 Company History

- (1) Launch Technologies Co., Ltd. (hereafter referred to as "the Company") was established in July 2006 and began operations in December 2006. Its main business is the manufacture and sale of golf products.  Advanced  International  Multitech  Co.,  Ltd.  holds  55.93%  of the Company's shares.
- (2) The Company's stock has been listed and traded on the Taipei Exchange since November 2014.
3. 五、 Date and procedure of approval through financial reports

This financial report was approved by the board of directors on February 22, 2023.

## 七、 Summary of Significant Accounting Policies

The  following  is  a  description  of  the  principal  accounting  policies adopted in the preparation of these financial statements. The following is  a  description  of  the  principal  accounting  policies  adopted  in  the preparation of these financial statements.

## ( 一 )Compliance statement.

These  financial  statements  have  been  prepared  in  accordance  with the  Financial  Reporting  Standards  for  Issuers  of  Securities  and Credit Rating Agencies, and the International Financial Reporting Standards, interpretations, and guidance issued and approved by the Financial Supervisory Commission, which have become effective.

## Basis of Preparation

Except for financial assets and liabilities (including derivative instruments) measured at fair value through profit or loss, the

financial statements are prepared on a historical cost basis.

The  preparation  of  financial  statements  in  compliance  with  IFRSs requires the use of significant accounting estimates and the exercise of judgment by management in the application of the Company's accounting policies, involving highly judgmental or complex  matters,  or  matters  involving  significant  assumptions  and estimates in the financial statements. Please refer to Note 5 for details.

## Foreign Currency Translation

The items in the financial statements of the Company are measured using the functional currency of the primary economic environment in  which  the  Company  operates.  The  financial  statements  are  presented  in  the  functional  currency  of  the  Company,  which  is  New Taiwan Dollar.

- Foreign currency transactions are translated into functional currency  at  the  spot  rate  on  the  transaction  date  or  measurement date. The resulting exchange differences are recognized in profit or loss for the period in which they arise.
- The foreign currency monetary assets and liabilities are measured at  the  spot  rate  on  the  balance  sheet  date  and  any  resulting exchange  differences  are  recognized  in  profit  or  loss  for  the period in which they arise.
- The balances of non-monetary assets and liabilities denominated in foreign currencies that are measured at fair value through profit or  loss  are  evaluated  at  the  spot  exchange  rate  on  the  balance sheet date for valuation adjustments, and the resulting exchange differences are recognized in the current period's profit or loss. For those measured at fair value through other comprehensive income, the resulting exchange differences due to adjustments made at the spot exchange rate on the balance sheet date are recognized in the other comprehensive income item. For those not measured at fair value, they are measured at the historical exchange rate on the initial transaction date.
- All exchange gains and losses are reported in the "Other gains and losses" item on the income statement.

## Classification standards for assets and liabilities as current or noncurrent

- 1.Assets are classified as current assets if they meet any of the

## following conditions:

- (1) The asset is expected to be realized during the normal operating cycle, or is intended to be sold or consumed.
- (2) The asset is held primarily for the purpose of trading.
- (3)  The  asset  is  expected  to  be  realized  within  twelve  months after the balance sheet date.
- (4) Cash or cash equivalents, except for those whose exchange or use for settlement of liabilities is restricted for at least twelve months after the balance sheet date.

All assets that do not meet the above conditions will be classified as non-current assets.

- 2.Liabilities  meeting  one  of  the  following  conditions  shall  be classified as current liabilities:
- (1) expected to be settled within the normal operating cycle.
- (2) The holder primarily holds for trading purposes.
- (3) expected to be settled within twelve months after the balance sheet date.
- (4)  cannot  unconditionally  defer  the  maturity  date  to  at  least twelve months after the balance sheet date. Liabilities that may be settled by issuing equity instruments at the option of the counterparty do not affect their classification.

All liabilities that do not meet the above criteria are classified as non-current.

## Equivalent to cash and cash equivalents

Cash equivalents refer to short-term and highly liquid investments that can be readily convertible to a known amount of cash and carry insignificant risk of changes in value. Time deposits that meet the above definition and are held to fulfill short-term cash commitments related to operations are classified as cash equivalents.

## Financial assets measured at fair value through profit or loss

- 1.Refers  to  financial  assets  not  measured  at  amortized  cost  or measured through other comprehensive income but measured at fair value through profit or loss.

The Company uses trade date accounting for financial assets measured at fair value through profit or loss that meet customary trading criteria.

The Company initially measures financial assets at fair value plus transaction costs, and subsequently accounts for them using the effective  interest  method  to  amortize  interest  income  over  the expected  life  of  the  financial  asset  and  recognize  impairment losses.  Any  gains  or  losses  on  derecognition  are  recognized  in profit or loss.

## Financial assets measured at amortized cost

- 1.Refers to financial assets that meet both of the following con-
- ditions:
- (1)  They  are  held  under  a  business  model  whose  objective  is  to hold assets to collect contractual cash flows.
- (2)  The  contractual  terms  of  the  financial  asset  give  rise  to cash  flows  on  specified  dates  that  are  solely  payments  of principal and interest on the principal amount outstanding.
- 2.For financial assets measured at amortized cost that meet market conventions, the Company uses trade date accounting.
- 3.The  company  initially  measures  these  financial  assets  at  fair value, including transaction costs. Subsequently, the effective interest method is used to recognize interest income during the period the financial assets are held and to recognize impairment losses. Any gains or losses upon derecognition are recognized in profit or loss.

## Accounts receivable and notes receivable

- 1.Refers  to  the  right  to  receive  payment  for  goods  or  services transferred,  which  is  an  unconditional  right  to  consideration that is specified in a contract.
- 2.Short-term accounts and notes receivable that are not interestbearing  and  are  not  significantly  impacted  by  discounting  are measured at the original invoice amount.

## Financial Asset Impairment

On each balance sheet date, the Company measures the allowance for impairment losses for financial assets measured at amortized cost, taking into account all reasonable and supportable information (including  forward-looking  information)  related  to  credit  risk  that has not increased significantly since initial recognition. For financial  assets  with  no  significant  increase  in  credit  risk  since initial recognition, the Company measures the expected credit loss amount for the next 12 months. For financial assets with a significant increase in credit risk since initial recognition, the Company measures the expected credit loss amount for the remaining life of

the  asset.  For  trade  receivables  or  contract  assets  that  do  not contain a significant financing component, the Company measures the expected credit loss amount for the remaining life of the asset.

## Disposal of Financial Assets

When the Company's contractual rights to receive cash flows from a financial asset have expired, the financial asset is derecognized.

## Inventory

Inventories are measured at cost or net realizable value, whichever  is  lower.  Cost  is  determined  using  the  weighted  average method  of  accounting.  The  cost  of  finished  goods  and  work  in progress includes raw materials, direct labor, other direct costs, and manufacturing overhead allocated based on normal production capacity, but excludes borrowing costs. When comparing cost and net  realizable  value,  the  individual  item  approach  is  adopted. Net realizable value refers to the estimated selling price in the normal course of business less the estimated costs of completion and estimated costs necessary to make the sale.

## Property, plant, and equipment

1.  Real  estate,  plants,  and  equipment  are  recorded  at  cost  and capitalized with related interest expenses incurred during the acquisition or construction period.
2. 2.Subsequent costs are only included in the carrying amount of an asset or recognized as a separate asset when it is highly probable  that  future  economic  benefits  associated  with  the  item will flow to the company and the cost of the item can be measured reliably.  The  carrying  amount  of  the  reset  portion  should  be derecognized. All other maintenance expenses are recognized in the statement of profit or loss when incurred.
3. 3.Real estate, plants, and equipment are measured using the cost model and depreciated on a straight-line basis over the estimated useful lives determined based on the estimated economic life. Depreciation of each significant component of real estate, plants, and equipment is separately provided.

The company reviews the residual values, useful lives, and depreciation methods of each asset at the end of each financial year. If  the  expected  values  of  residual  values  and  useful  lives differ from previous estimates, or there is a significant change in the expected consumption pattern of future economic benefits of  the  asset,  the  accounting  estimate  change  is  handled  in

accordance  with  International  Accounting  Standard  No.  8  "Ac- counting Policies, Changes in Accounting Estimates and Errors" from  the  date  of  change.  The  estimated  useful  lives  of  each asset are as follows:

Buildings and structures

3 ～ 50 years

Machinery and equipment

3 ～ 9 years

Water and electricity equipment

1 ～ 16 years

Transportation equipment

6 years

Office equipment

1 ～ 6 years

Other equipment

1 ～ 16 years

## Lessee's Lease Transactions - Right-of-use Assets/Lease Liabilities

- 1.The lease assets are recognized as right-of-use assets and lease liabilities on the date when they are available for the company to  use.  When  the  lease  contract  is  a  short-term  lease  or  the lease involves low-value assets, the lease payments are recognized as expenses on a straight-line basis over the lease term.
- 2.The lease liabilities are recognized on the lease commencement date for the present value of the lease payments not yet paid, discounted  at  the  incremental  borrowing  rate  of  the  company. Lease payments include:
- (1) Fixed payments, excluding any lease incentives receivable;
- (2) Variable lease payments dependent on an index or rate;
- (3) Amounts expected to be payable by the company under residual value guarantees; and
- (4) Penalties for the termination of the lease that the lessee is  expected  to  pay  if  the  lessee  exercises  an  option  to terminate the lease during the lease term.

Subsequent to initial recognition, the assets are measured using the  interest  method  and  amortized  cost  method,  and  interest expense  is  accrued  over  the  lease  term.  When  a  lease  term  or lease payment is modified without being accounted for as a lease modification,  the  lease  liabilities  are  remeasured,  and  the right-of-use assets are adjusted accordingly.

3.  The  right-of-use  asset  is  recognized  at  cost  on  the  lease commencement date, which includes:

- (1) The original carrying amount of lease liabilities;
- (2) Any lease payments made on or before the commencement date;
- (3) Any initial direct costs incurred; and
- (4) The estimated costs of dismantling, removing the underlying asset and restoring the site on which it is located, or of restoring the underlying asset to the condition required by the terms and conditions of the lease.

Subsequently  measured  using  the  cost  model,  depreciation  expense shall be recognized when the useful life of the right-ofuse  asset  or  the  lease  term,  whichever  is  earlier,  expires. When  lease  liabilities  are remeasured, any  re-measurement amount  of  the  lease  liability  shall  adjust  the  right-of-use asset.

## Intangible Assets

Computer  software  is  recognized  at  cost  and  amortized  over  its estimated useful life of 5 years using the straight-line method.

## Impairment of non-financial assets.

For assets with indications of impairment as of the balance sheet date, the recoverable amount is estimated, and an impairment loss is recognized when the recoverable amount is less than the carrying amount. The recoverable amount refers to the higher of an asset's fair value less disposal costs and its value in use. When the conditions for the recognition of asset impairment losses in previous  years  no  longer  exist  or  have  decreased,  impairment losses are reversed. However, the increase in the carrying amount of assets resulting from the reversal of impairment losses shall not exceed the carrying amount of the asset that would have been determined had no impairment loss been recognized in prior years, less depreciation or amortization.

## Borrowings

Borrowings  refer  to  long-term  and  short-term  amounts  borrowed from banks. At initial recognition, borrowing costs are measured ference  between  the  redemption  value  and  the  price  paid,  less transaction  costs,  is  recognized  as  interest  expense  using  the at fair value less transaction costs, and subsequently, any difeffective interest method over the period of the borrowing.

## Accounts Payable and Notes Payable

- 1.Refers  to  debts  incurred  for  the  purchase  of  raw  materials,

goods, or services on credit.

- 2.These are short-term accounts payable and notes payable without interest, and the Company measures them at the original invoice amount as the impact of discounting is immaterial.

## Convertible Company Bonds Payable

The convertible company bonds payable issued by the Company are embedded with conversion rights (i.e., the holder has the right to convert the bonds into common shares of the Company at a fixed conversion  rate)  and  call  options.  At  initial  recognition,  the issue price is allocated as financial assets or equity based on the terms of issuance, as follows:

- 1.Embedded call options: At initial recognition, the net amount of the fair value of the call option is recognized as "financial assets at fair value through profit or loss." Subsequently, any change in fair value is recognized as "gain or loss on financial assets at fair value through profit or loss" in the statement of income at each balance sheet date.
- 2.Main contract of the bonds: At initial recognition, the bonds are  measured  at  fair  value,  and  any  difference  between  the redemption value and the initial carrying amount is recognized as  a  discount  on  the  bonds  payable.  Subsequently,  using  the effective interest rate method, the discount is amortized over the term of the bonds and recognized as an adjustment item for "financial cost."
- 3.Embedded conversion rights (meeting the definition of equity): At initial recognition, the residual value after deducting the above "financial assets at fair value through profit or loss" and "bonds payable" from the issue price is recognized as "capital  surplus  -  stock  option,"  and  is  not  re-measured  subsequently.
- 4.Any direct transaction costs incurred in the issuance are allocated  to  the  various  components  of  liabilities  and  equity based on the proportion of the original carrying amount of each component.
- 5.When the holder converts the bonds, the portion of the liability component (including "bonds payable" and "financial assets at fair value through profit or loss") is re-measured based on the subsequent measurement method for each component. The carrying amount of the equity component "capital surplus - stock option" is then increased by the carrying amount of the liability component to determine the issuance cost of the common shares.

## Exclusion of financial liabilities

When the company fulfills, cancels or expires its obligations as stated in the contract, financial liabilities are excluded.

## Non-hedging derivative instruments

Non-hedging derivative instruments are measured at fair value on the  date  of  contract  signing  upon  initial  recognition,  and  are accounted for as financial assets or liabilities measured at fair value through profit or loss. Subsequently, gains or losses are recognized in profit or loss.

## Employee benefits

## 1.Short-term employee benefits

Short-term  employee  benefits  are  measured  at  the  expected non-discounted amount to be paid and recognized as an expense when the related services are provided.

## 2.Retirement benefits

Retirement  benefits,  which  are  defined  contribution  plans, are recognized as retirement benefit costs for the period in which the related obligations are incurred based on the obligation  to  make  contributions.  Prepaid  contributions  are recognized as assets within the scope of refundable cash or future reductions in benefits.

## Severance benefits

The benefits provided for employees upon termination of employment  prior  to  the  normal  retirement  date  or  upon  the acceptance of a company's invitation for a benefit in exchange for  the  termination  of  employment  shall  be  recognized  as  a liability. The Company shall recognize the expenses when it is no longer able to revoke the offer of termination benefits or  at  the  earlier  of  recognition  of  related  restructuring costs.  The  benefits  that  are  not  expected  to  be  settled  in full within 12 months after the balance sheet date shall be discounted.

## Employee and Director/Supervisor Remuneration

Employee  and  Director/Supervisor  remuneration  is  recognized as  an  expense  and  liability  when  it  is  legally  required  or presumed  and  the  amount  can  be  reasonably  estimated.  Any difference  between  the  actual  distribution  amount  and  the estimated  amount  shall  be  handled  according  to  accounting estimates. Employee remuneration may also be granted in the

## Income tax

form of stocks, and the basis for calculating equity is the closing price one day prior to the Board of Directors' resolution.

- 1.Income tax expenses include current and deferred income taxes. Except  for  income  taxes  related  to  items  included  in  other comprehensive income or directly included in equity, income tax is recognized in profit or loss.
- 2.The Company calculates income tax rates based on laws already enacted or substantively enacted on the balance sheet date. Management  evaluates  the  status  of  income  tax  reporting  in accordance with relevant income tax regulations and estimates income tax liabilities based on the expected tax payable to the  tax  authorities  under  applicable  circumstances.  Income tax on undistributed earnings levied under the income tax law shall be recognized as an expense for undistributed earnings after the next year following the year in which the earnings were generated upon approval of the dividend distribution at the shareholder meeting.

- 3.Deferred  income  tax  is  recognized  using  the  balance  sheet method,  recognizing  temporary  differences  between  the  tax basis  of  assets  and  liabilities  and  their  carrying  amounts in the balance sheet. Deferred income tax liabilities arising from goodwill originally recognized shall not be recognized, and if deferred income tax arises from the original recognition of assets or liabilities in a transaction that did not affect accounting profit or taxable income (tax loss) at the time of the transaction, it shall not be recognized. Deferred income  tax  is  based  on  the  tax  rates  (and  laws)  that  are expected to be applied when the related deferred income tax assets  are  realized  or  the  deferred  income  tax  liabilities are settled, using the tax rates already enacted or substantively enacted on the balance sheet date.
- 4.Deferred  income  tax  assets  may  be  recognized  for  temporary differences that are likely to be utilized to offset future taxable income, and deferred income tax assets that have been recognized and those that have not been recognized should be reassessed on each balance sheet date.
- 5.When there is a legal right to offset the amounts of current income  tax  assets  and  liabilities  recognized  for  the  same taxable entity with the intention of settling on a net basis or simultaneously realizing assets and settling liabilities, the current income tax assets and liabilities should be offset. When  there  is  a  legal  right  to  offset  current  income  tax assets  and  liabilities,  and  the  deferred  income  tax  assets and  liabilities  arise  from  the  same  tax  authority  levying income  tax  on  the  same  taxable  entity  or  different  taxable entities  but  with  the  intention  of  settling  on  a  net  basis or simultaneously realizing assets and settling liabilities, the  deferred  income  tax  assets  and  liabilities  should  be offset.

## Share Capital

Common stocks are classified as equity.

## Dividend distribution.

Cash  dividends  distributed  to  the  Company's  shareholders  are recognized as liabilities in the financial statements when approved by the Board of Directors.

## Recognition of revenue.

- 1.The Company manufactures and sells consumer products. Sales

revenue is recognized when control of the product is transferred to the customer, which is when the product is delivered to the customer and the Company has no remaining obligations that could affect the customer's acceptance of the product. The point of delivery occurs when the risks of obsolescence, damage,  or  loss  have  been  transferred  to  the  customer  upon shipment, the customer has accepted the product according to the sales contract, or there is objective evidence that all acceptance criteria have been met.

- Sales revenue is recognized as the net amount of contract price minus  business  tax,  sales  returns,  quantity  discounts,  and allowances.  Payment  terms  for  sales  transactions  are  due within  30  to  90  days  after  the  shipment  date.  As  the  time period between the transfer of goods or services to the customer and the receipt of payment from the customer does not exceed one year, the Company does not adjust the transaction price to reflect the time value of money.
- 3.Accounts receivable are recognized when the products are delivered  to  the  customer,  as  at  that  point,  the  Company  has an  unconditional  right  to  receive  payment  at  the  contract price, and only the passage of time is required for the customer to remit payment.

## Government Grants

Government grants are recognized at fair value when it is probable  that  the  enterprise  will  comply  with  the  conditions  attached to the grants and the grants will be received.

## Operating Segment

The information on operating segments of the Company provided to the primary operating decision-makers is reported in a consistent manner in the internal management reports. The primary operating  decision-makers  are  responsible  for  allocating  resources to the operating segments and assessing their performance.

## 八、 Major Sources of Estimation Uncertainty and Significant Accounting Judgments and Assumptions

In preparing these financial statements, management has made judgments in  determining  accounting  policies  and  made  accounting  estimates  and assumptions  based  on  reasonable  expectations  of  future  events  and  on

the  basis  of  the  circumstances  prevailing  at  the  balance  sheet  date. These estimates and assumptions may differ from actual results and are continuously  evaluated  and  adjusted,  taking  into  account  historical experience and other factors. These estimates and assumptions may create a  risk  of  material  adjustments  to  the  carrying  amounts  of  assets  and liabilities  in  the  next  accounting  year.  Please  provide  detailed  explanations on the uncertainty of significant accounting estimates and assumptions:

Significant Accounting Estimates and Assumptions

## 1.stimation of allowance for doubtful accounts receivable

The Company measures the allowance for doubtful accounts receivable based  on  the  expected  credit  losses  over  the  remaining  period.  In measuring the expected credit losses, the Company must exercise judgment  to  identify  the  factors  that  may  affect  the  recoverability  of the accounts receivable, such as the customer's operating status and historical transaction records that may affect their payment ability, while considering the time value of money and reasonable and supportable  information  on  future  economic  conditions.  The  aforementioned judgment and consideration factors may significantly impact the measurement results of expected losses.

As of December 31, 2022, the carrying amount of the Company's accounts receivable is $500,928.

## 2.Valuation of Inventory

As inventory is valued at the lower of cost or net realizable value, the  Company  must  exercise  judgment  and  estimation  to  determine  the net realizable value of inventory on the balance sheet date. Due to rapid technological changes, the Company evaluates the amount of inventory on the balance sheet date that is subject to normal wear and tear,  obsolescence,  or  lack  of  market  sales  value  and  reduces  the inventory cost to the net realizable value. This inventory valuation is primarily based on the estimation of product demand within a specific future period, which may result in significant changes.

As  of  December  31,  2022,  the  inventory  of  the  Company  amounted  to $690,249.

## Corporate bonds payable

| December 31, 2022  Corporate bonds payable  $          500,000  Less: Discount on company bonds payable  (            29,926)  470,074  Less: Company bonds payable due within one year  -  $          470,074   |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

As of December 31, 2021 ： No such event occurred.

- 1.Domestic unsecured convertible bonds issued by the Company
- (1) Issuance terms are as follows:
- A. The Company received approval from the regulatory authority to raise funds and issue the first domestic unsecured convertible bonds at a face value of $500,000 at 102.98% of  the  face  value,  with  a  total  fundraising  amount  of $514,901, a coupon rate of 0%, a three-year issuance period, and  a  trading  period  from  October  7,  2022  to  October  7, 114  (2025).  On  the  business  day  following  the  maturity date of the convertible bonds, the bonds shall be redeemed in cash in a lump sum according to the face value of the bonds.  The  convertible  bonds  were  listed  for  trading  on the Taiwan Stock Exchange on October 7, 2022.
- Broom the day after the three-month anniversary of the issuance  date  of  the  bonds  (January  8,  2023),  until  the maturity date, unless the transfer of ownership is temporarily suspended due to regulations or laws, the bondholders  may  request  to  convert  the  bonds  into  common  shares of the Company at any time, with the same rights and obligations as those of the existing common shares.

- C. The conversion price of the convertible bonds is determined based on the pricing model specified in the conversion rules. The conversion price at the time of issuance is NT$75 per share. Subsequently, if there is a situation where the Company is subject to anti-dilution provisions, the  conversion  price  will  be  adjusted  according  to  the pricing model specified in the conversion rules. Then, on the basis date specified in the rules, the conversion price will be redefined according to the pricing model specified in the conversion rules. If the newly defined conversion price is higher than the conversion price before the redefinition  in  the  same  fiscal  year,  no  adjustment  shall be made.
- D. From the next day after three months of the issuance of the  convertible  corporate  bonds  (January  8,  2023)  until 40 days before the end of the issuance period (August 28, 2025), if the closing price of common stocks of the Company exceeds the conversion price by more than 30% for 30 consecutive business days, or if the outstanding balance of the  convertible  corporate  bonds  is  less  than  10%  of  the original issuance amount during the same period, the Company may redeem all the bonds in cash at face value at any time thereafter.
- E.  According  to  the  conversion  rules,  all  the  convertible corporate bonds that the Company redeems (including those repurchased  by  the  OTC  market),  repays,  or  has  already converted will be canceled, and all the rights and obligations  attached  to  the  bonds  will  also  be  extinguished and will no longer be issued.
- (2)  As  of  December  31,  2022,  there  has  been  no  conversion  of the  convertible  corporate  bonds  into  common  stocks  or  adjustment of the conversion price.
- (3)  As  of  December  31,  2022,  the  Company  has  not  repurchased any of the convertible corporate bonds through the OTC market.
6. 2.When issuing convertible corporate bonds, the Company separates the conversion rights, which are equity instruments, from the liability components in accordance with International Accounting Standard 32 "Financial Instruments: Presentation," and records them as "Capital Surplus - Stock Subscription Rights" for $42,611. The embedded repurchase option is separated in accordance with International Financial Reporting Standard 9 "Financial  Instruments"  because  its  economic  characteristics  and risks  are  not  closely  related  to  the  host  contract  liability

instrument, and the net amount is recorded as "Financial assets at fair value through profit or loss." The effective interest rate of the host contract liability after separation is 2.2563%.

## Retirement benefits

- 1.Starting from July 1, 2005, the Company has established a defined contribution retirement plan in accordance with the Labor Pension Act, applicable to its employees who are nationals of Taiwan. For the portion of the retirement plan that is subject to  the  Labor  Pension  Act,  the  Company  contributes  6%  of  the employees' salaries to the individual accounts of the employees with the Bureau of Labor Insurance. The payment of retirement benefits to the employees will be made in the form of monthly annuity payments or lump sum payments, depending on the accumulated amount in the employees' individual accounts.
- 2.For  the  years  ended  December  31,  2022  and  2021,  the  Company recognized  retirement  benefit  costs  of  $18,393  and  $13,975,
- respectively, in accordance with the above retirement plan.

## Capital surplus

According to the Company Law, the capital surplus from the issuance of shares exceeding the face value and the capital reserve from the receipt of gifts can be used to issue new shares or cash, in  proportion  to  the  original  shareholders'  shares,  except  for compensating for losses when there are no accumulated losses in the company. In addition, according to the relevant provisions of the  Securities  and  Exchange  Act,  when  the  capital  reserve  is allocated to the capital, the total amount shall not exceed 10% of the paid-in capital each year. When there is still insufficient capital  to  offset  capital  losses  using  the  surplus  of  earnings reserves, it cannot be supplemented with capital reserves.

## Retained earnings

1. According to the company's articles of association, if there is a profit in the annual settlement, it shall be distributed in the following order: (1) payment of income tax, (2) offsetting  accumulated  losses,  (3)  depositing  10%  as  the  statutory surplus  reserve,  but  if  the  statutory  surplus  reserve  has reached the total capital amount, this does not apply, (4) the special surplus reserve allocated or reversed by the shareholders' meeting or the competent authority, and (5) the remaining balance after the current year's allocation plus the undistributed profits at the beginning of the period shall be the accumulated distributable profits of the shareholders. The board of directors  shall  propose  a  distribution  plan  and  submit  it  to the shareholders' meeting for approval. The distribution amount shall not be less than 50% of the distributable profits for the year.
2. The company considers the business environment and growth prospects, future funding needs and long-term financial planning, and to meet the shareholders' demand for cash dividends. Among the dividends distributed from profit allocation, the cash dividends shall not be less than 10% of the total dividend amount.
3.  The  company  authorizes  the  board  of  directors,  with  the  at-

tendance of more than two-thirds of the directors and the majority of the attending directors, to distribute all or part of the  dividends,  capital  surplus,  or  statutory  surplus  reserve through the issuance of cash, and report to the shareholders' meeting. This provision does not apply to the requirement for approval by the shareholders' meeting.

4. The statutory surplus reserve can only be used to offset company  losses  or  issue  new  shares  or  cash  in  proportion  to  the original shareholders' shares, and the amount of such shares or cash shall not exceed 25% of the surplus reserve amount exceeding 25% of the paid-in capital.
5. When the company distributes profits, it must first allocate the special surplus reserve by debiting the other equity items on the balance sheet date of the current year, as required by laws  and  regulations,  and  then  distribute  it.  The  reversed amount  of  the  other  equity  items  can  be  included  in  the  distributable profits.
6. The shareholders' meeting passed the profit distribution plan for the 110th fiscal year in May 2022, and the profit distribution plan for the 109th fiscal year was passed through electronic voting before June 30, 2021, reaching the threshold for statutory approval. The shareholders' meeting passed the profit distribution plan for the 109th fiscal year on July 12, 2021, as follows:

## Income tax

- 1.According to the Company's Articles of Association, if the Company makes a profit in the current year, it shall distribute employee compensation of no less than 1% and director compensation not exceeding 5%. However, if the Company still has accumulated losses, they shall be compensated first. Before the establishment of the Company's Audit Committee, the remuneration of supervisors shall be distributed in accordance with the aforementioned ratio.
- 2.The estimated amounts of employee remuneration for the 2022 and 2021 fiscal years of the Company were $41,600 and $18,675, respectively, and the estimated amounts of director and supervisor remuneration were $4,500 and $3,000, respectively. The aforementioned amounts are recorded under the salary expenses item.

The estimated amounts for the 2022 fiscal year were based on a  certain  ratio  according  to  the  profitability  up  to  that period.

The employee and director and supervisor remuneration for the 2021 fiscal year resolved by the Board of Directors are consistent  with  the  amounts  recognized  in  the  2021  financial report. Employee compensation is paid in cash.

Information on employee compensation and director and supervisor compensation approved by the Company's Board of Directors can be obtained from the Public Information Observation System.

## 十五、 Others

## (1) Capital management

The Company's capital management objective is to ensure the Company's continued operation, maintain an optimal capital structure to reduce funding costs, and provide returns to shareholders. To maintain or adjust the capital structure, the Company may adjust the amount of dividends paid to shareholders, return capital to shareholders,  issue  new  shares,  or  sell  assets  to  reduce  debt. The  Company  monitors  its  capital  by  using  the  debt-to-capital ratio, which is calculated by dividing net debt by total capital.

The  capital  management  strategy  of  the  Company  in  2022  is  the same as that of 2021, which is committed to balancing the overall capital structure. The debt-to-capital ratios of the Company as of December 31, 2022 and 2021 are as follows:

## 2.Risk Management Policies

- (1) Our company's daily operations are exposed to various financial risks, including market risks (including foreign exchange  risk,  interest  rate  risk,  and  price  risk),  credit risk, and liquidity risk. Our overall risk management policy

- focuses  on  unforeseeable  events  in  financial  markets  and seeks to mitigate potential adverse impacts on our financial condition and performance.
- (2)  Risk  management  work  is  executed  by  the  Finance  and  Accounting Department of our company in accordance with policies.  The  Finance  and  Accounting  Department  works  closely with  business  units  of  the  company  to  identify,  evaluate, and  mitigate  financial  risks.  Our  company  has  established written principles for overall risk management, and provides written policies for specific areas and issues, such as exchange rate risk, interest rate risk, credit risk, the use of derivative and non-derivative financial instruments, and the investment of remaining liquid funds.
- (3) Nature and degree of significant financial risks
3. Nature and degree of significant financial risks.
- (1) Market risk

## Exchange rate risk

- A. As an international operating company, our main exchange rate risk comes from the US dollar and other currencies. The related exchange rate risk arises from future business transactions and recognized assets and liabilities.
2. Boor management has established policies to manage exchange rate risk relative to its functional currency. The company hedges its overall exchange rate risk through the Finance and  Accounting  Department.  To  manage  exchange  rate  risk from  future  business  transactions  and  recognized  assets and liabilities, the company uses forward foreign exchange contracts through the Finance and Accounting Department. When future business transactions, recognized assets, or liabilities are denominated in non-functional currencies of the entity, exchange rate risk arises.
- C.  Our  company's  business  involves  several  non-functional currencies (the functional currency of our company is the New  Taiwan  Dollar),  so  it  is  affected  by  exchange  rate fluctuations. The information on significant foreign currency assets and liabilities impacted by significant exchange rate fluctuations is as follows:

## Price Risk

The Company is not exposed to price risk on any commodity. Cash flow and fair value interest rate risk

The  Company's  interest  rate  risk  arises  primarily  from floating  rate  borrowings,  both  short-term  and  long-term, which exposes the Company to cash flow interest rate risk.

The  floating  rate  borrowings  issued  by  the  Company  in  the 2022  and  2021  fiscal  years  were  mainly  denominated  in  New Taiwan  Dollars.  If  the  interest  rate  on  New  Taiwan  Dollar borrowings were to increase or decrease by 0.25% while all other factors remain constant, the net profit after tax for the 111th and 110th fiscal years would decrease or increase by $215 and $474, respectively, mainly due to the fluctuation of interest expenses resulting from floating rate borrowings.

## (2) Credit Risk

Credit risk is the risk of financial loss that the Company incurs due to the counterparty's failure to fulfill contractual obligations in transactions with customers or financial instruments. The internal risk control is evaluated by considering the financial condition, past experience, and other factors to assess the credit quality of customers.

## Cash and cash equivalents, derivative financial instruments, and financial assets measured at amortized cost.

Due  to  the  Company's  transaction  policy,  which  stipulates only  transactions  with  good  credit  rating  parties,  there have been no significant defaults in cash and cash equivalents,  derivative  financial  instruments,  and  financial  assets measured at amortized cost.

## Accounts receivable

- A. In accordance with the internal credit policy, the company conducts management and credit risk analysis for each new customer before setting payment and delivery terms and conditions. The internal risk control is evaluated by considering  the  financial  condition,  past  experience,  and other factors to assess the credit quality of customers.
- B. The company assumes that if contract payments are overdue for  more  than  90  days  according  to  the  agreed  payment terms, the credit risk has significantly increased since initial recognition of financial assets. If contract payments are overdue for more than 180 days, it is considered a default.
- C. The company's accounts receivable are mainly from general enterprises. The company evaluates the credit quality of each  customer  by  considering  multiple  factors  that  may affect their payment ability, such as financial condition, transaction history, and current economic status, using a simplified  approach  based  on  the  preparation  matrix  to estimate expected credit losses.
- D. After the collection process, the company will write off the amount of financial assets that cannot be reasonably expected to be recovered. However, the company will continue to pursue legal proceedings to preserve its rights to recover the debt.
- E.  The  expected  loss  rates  for  the  company's  accounts  receivable from customers as of December 31, 2012 and 2022 are as follows:

## (3) Liquidity risk

- A.  The  cash  flow  forecast  is  executed  by  the  Finance  and Accounting Department of the company to ensure sufficient funds to support operational needs and maintain sufficient unused loan commitment limits.

- B. Any excess cash held by the Company beyond its working capital management needs is invested in interest-bearing current  deposits  and  cash  equivalents  with  appropriate maturities or sufficient liquidity to meet the above forecast  and  provide  adequate  headroom.  As  of  December  31, 2022 and 2021, the Company held monetary market positions of $725,085 and $131,659, respectively, which are expected to generate immediate cash flow to manage liquidity risk.

## (3)

## Fair value information

- 1.The  definitions  of  various  levels  of  valuation  techniques adopted to measure the fair value of financial and non-financial instruments are as follows:
- Level 1: The quoted prices (unadjusted) in an active market for identical assets or liabilities that the entity can obtain at the measurement date. An active market is a market in which transactions for the asset or liability occur with sufficient frequency and volume to provide pricing information on an ongoing basis.
- Level 2: It refers to direct or indirect observable inputs of assets or liabilities, except quoted prices included in Level 1. The fair value of forward foreign exchange contracts invested by the Company is included.
- Level 3: Unobservable inputs of assets or liabilities. The Company's  convertible  bonds  redemption  rights  are  included.
- 2.The carrying amounts of financial instruments not measured at fair value by the Company (including cash and cash equivalents, financial assets measured at amortized cost, accounts receivable,  notes  receivable,  other  receivables,  deposits  received, short-term borrowings, notes payable, accounts payable, other payables, lease liabilities, and long-term borrowings) represent a reasonable approximation of their fair values.

7. The Company's process for evaluating fair value classified in Level 3 is carried out by the Finance Department. The Company regularly calibrates the valuation model, performs back-testing, updates input values and data required for the valuation model, and makes any necessary fair value adjustments to ensure that the results are reasonable.

## (4) Other Matters

The Company's main customer base is in the United States, Japan, and other regions. Its products mainly consist of outdoor leisure sports products that can maintain appropriate social distancing. There  is  no  significant  reduction  in  operating  scale  or  endconsumer demand due to the epidemic. The Company has also complied with  the  guidelines  of  the  Taiwan  Centers  for  Disease  Control, including  personnel  access  control,  production  area  and  shift separation control measures. After evaluation, the COVID-19 pandemic  has  not  significantly  affected  the  Company's  operational status and financial performance.

## 十七、 Segment information

## ( 一

## )General information

The company operates only in a single industry, and the company's management evaluates performance and allocates resources based on the overall operating results of the company. Therefore, the company has been identified as a single reporting segment.

## (2)

## Measurement of Departmental Information.

The Board of Directors of the company evaluates the performance

of  operating  segments  based  on  their  tax-deductible  income  or loss  from  continuing  operations.  This  measurement  standard  excludes the impact of non-recurring items in the operating segments. Interest income and expense are not allocated to operating segments because such activities are managed by the accounting and finance department responsible for the company's cash position.

## (3) Information on segment income or loss, assets, and liabilities.

As the company is a single reporting segment, the financial statements prepared in accordance with generally accepted accounting principles  are  provided  as  segment  information  to  the  primary operating decision-makers.

## (4) Adjusted information for segment income or loss.

The revenue reported to the primary operating decision-makers is measured  consistently  with  the  revenue  reported  in  the  income statement.

## (5) Information on product and service segments.

## 1. Policy for the allocation of compensation to directors and supervisors:

In addition to distributing dividends not exceeding 5% according to the company's Articles of Association, the remuneration of the company's directors and supervisors will be determined comprehensively based on the company's actual operating results, future business risks and development, and considering the same industry level. The remuneration will be approved by the Remuneration Committee, the Board of Directors, and reported to the shareholders' meeting for issuance.

## 2.Managerial Compensation Policy:

## (1) Policy, Standards, and Composition of Compensation

The compensation for managers in our company includes salaries, bonuses, and employee benefits, which are determined according to the relevant regulations of our company.

Salaries and bonuses are distributed after review by the Compensation Committee and approval by the Board of Directors, while employee benefits are distributed after being reported to the shareholders' meeting. The policy for paying compensation takes into account the overall performance of the company and the contribution of the managers to the company's achievements.

## (2) Procedures for Determining Compensation

In addition to evaluating factors such as education, experience, length of service, and job characteristics of the managers, the compensation they receive is determined based on the actual operating performance and contribution of the company each year.

## Launch Technologies CO., LTD. Detailed List of Employee Benefits, Depreciation and Amortization Expenses (Continued) Fiscal Year 2022

Unit: NT$ thousands

Detailed List 17

Unit: NT$ thousands

The procedures for determining compensation are carried out in accordance with the "Salary Management Operations Procedure Manual," "Year-end and Annual Bonus Distribution Procedure Manual," "Performance Appraisal Operations Procedure Manual," and "Employee Benefits and Director and Supervisor Compensation Distribution Regulations."

All compensation for managers in our company must be reviewed by the Compensation Committee and approved by the Board of Directors before being distributed.

## (3) Relationship with Management Performance and Future Risks

In addition to considering the overall performance of the company and the future risks and developments, the compensation for managers in our company is also based on the general level of compensation in the industry or market and the responsibility, contribution, and performance of the managers. The company's management and the Compensation Committee will regularly review and make appropriate adjustments to the compensation policy to recognize the responsibilities and risks they undertake.

- (I) Impact of interest rates, exchange rates, and inflation on the company's income and measures to be taken in the future.

(loss)

- (1) Interest rate risk: The market interest rate fluctuation in the latest fiscal year was not significant, and it is not expected to generate significant market interest rate fluctuations. The company has a dedicated person to regularly evaluate bank loan interest rates and maintain good communication with banks to obtain preferential loan interest rates, thus it is expected that there will be no significant market interest rate fluctuation risks.
- (2) Exchange rate risk: The company's main transactions are denominated in US dollars, and the exchange rate risk will be reduced after offsetting foreign currency receivables  and  payables.  The  company  has  a  clear  foreign  exchange  operating strategy and a dedicated person to monitor foreign exchange fluctuations and engage in forward exchange transactions to mitigate possible risks. Therefore, the impact of exchange rate fluctuations on its income is also maintained within a certain range.
- (3) Inflation: The company keeps a close eye on market price trends and adopts flexible strategies to actively coordinate with suppliers and reduce the impact of inflation.
4. (II) Policies, main reasons for profit or loss, and future response measures for engaging in high-risk, high-leverage investments, lending funds to others, endorsing guarantees, and trading in derivative financial products are analyzed and evaluated on an annual basis and up until the date of the annual report.
- (1) The company does not engage in high-risk, high-leverage investments.
- (2) Currently, the company has not endorsed guarantees for others or lent funds to others. If the need arises to endorse guarantees or lend funds to others in the future, the relevant procedures and response measures will be carried out in accordance with the regulations of the regulatory authorities and the company's "Operation Procedures for Lending Funds to Others" and "Operation Procedures for Endorsing Guarantees," and all transaction information will be disclosed in accordance with the law and regulations.
- (3) The company handles the acquisition or disposal of assets in accordance with the regulations of the regulatory authorities and the company's "Asset Acquisition or Disposal Processing Procedures" and relevant response measures. The derivative financial products currently traded by the company are mainly forward foreign exchange contracts signed with financial institutions to avoid the risk of exchange rate fluctuations.

- (IV) Impact of Important Changes in Domestic and International Policies and Regulations on Corporate Finance and Sales, and Response Strategies:

The Company constantly monitors important domestic and international policies and legal changes, evaluates their impact on the Company, and takes responsive measures.

- (V) The impact of technological changes (including cybersecurity risks) and industry transformations on the Company's financial operations and corresponding measures are considered.

Under the Industry 4.0 trend, the Company closely monitors the latest changes in related industries and introduces new technologies to increase competitiveness and provide top-quality services as future goals.

With the development of information technology, the challenges of information security faced by companies are becoming increasingly severe. To reduce information security risks, the Company has established an information security risk management framework, formulated an information security policy, and taken concrete management actions to protect the Company's important assets and information.

- (VI) The Company has long been committed to improving and maintaining its image, and to date, there have been no incidents that have significantly affected the Company's image. If such an incident were to occur, a special project team would be immediately formed to respond. There are no expected benefits, potential risks, or corresponding measures for mergers and acquisitions.
- (VII)Expected benefits, potential risks, and response measures of mergers and acquisitions: None.

(VIII)Expected benefits, potential risks, and response measures of expanding the factory:

Expanding the factory can enhance the company's production capacity to take on more customer orders, thereby increasing revenue and profits and potentially expanding market share. After reaching the economic scale of production capacity, it can also significantly reduce production costs.

However, when the economy slows down and market demand decreases, the risk of idle capacity still needs to be allocated to the depreciation of plant and equipment, which will become one of the company's burdens.

The expansion of the company's factories has gone through a complete, prudent, and dedicated evaluation process, striving to meet customer needs while fully considering the investment return benefits and potential risks in order to optimize capital utilization.

- (IX) The risks in centralized purchases and sales, and corresponding strategies
- (1) Purchases:

The company's main raw materials are rubber and various chemical materials, which have abundant supply sources and diverse supplier options both domestically and internationally. The company maintains good cooperation relationships with various suppliers to ensure a stable supply source.

- (2) Sales

The company's main product is golf balls, and it is an important professional OEM manufacturer in the market. With advantages in technology, quality, and price flexibility, the company has cooperation relationships with several important golf brand manufacturers globally, with customers in the United States, Japan, Europe, and other regions. The company has a diverse customer base, and although the sales proportion to a single customer exceeded 50% in 2022, this is due to the industry's characteristics where the market sales are concentrated in a few brand manufacturers. The company actively expands opportunities for cooperation with other brands, develops distribution channels and customers, and expands its product line to further reduce sales dependency risks.

- (X) As of the printing date of the annual report for the year 2022, there was no significant transfer of shares or change of ownership by directors, supervisors, or major shareholders holding more than 10% of the company's shares.
- (XI) There is no impact, risk, or contingency measures regarding a change in management control of the company.
- (XII) Litigation or non-litigation events: The Company should disclose any significant litigation, non-litigation, or administrative disputes that have been adjudicated or are currently  pending  involving  the  Company, its  directors,  supervisors,  general  manager, substantial shareholders with a shareholding of more than 10%, or subsidiary companies, and may have a significant impact on shareholders' equity or security prices. The disclosure  should  include  information  on  the  nature  of  the  dispute,  the  amount  involved, the date of the dispute, the primary parties involved, and the status of the case as of the date of the annual report: None.
- (XIII) Other significant risks and countermeasures: None.
